Shellability of 3 3 -cut complexes of powers of cycle graphs
Pratiksha Chauhan, Samir Shukla

Abstract
. In connection with commutative algebra, Bayer et al. introduced cut complexes in [Topology of cut complexes of graphs, SIAM J. Discrete Math., 38(2):1630-1675, 2024]. For a positive integer k k , the k k -cut complex of a graph G G , denoted as Δ k ​ ( G ) \Delta_{k}(G) , is the simplicial complex whose facets are the ( | V ⁡ ( G ) | − k ) (|V(G)|-k) -subsets σ \sigma of the vertex set V ⁡ ( G ) V(G) of G G such that the induced subgraph G ⁡ [ V ⁡ ( G ) ∖ σ ] G[V(G)\setminus\sigma] is disconnected. Let C n p C_{n}^{p} denote the p p -th power graph of the cycle graph C n C_{n} on n n vertices. In this article, we show that Δ 3 ​ ( C n p ) \Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}) is shellable for n ≥ 6 ​ p − 3 n\geq 6p-3 , and therefore these complexes are homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres of dimension n − 4 n-4 . We provide an explicit shelling order on the facets of Δ 3 ​ ( C n p ) \Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}) . We also characterize and count the number of spanning facets in this shelling order, and determine the number of spheres appearing in the wedge in the homotopy type of Δ 3 ​ ( C n p ) \Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}) .



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
57M15, 52B22, 55U05, 05C69, 05E45

1.
Introduction

In this article, all graphs are assumed to be finite and simple (that is, without loops or multiple edges). The vertex set and edge set of a graph
G
are denoted by
V(G)
and
E(G)
, respectively.

A graph complex is a simplicial complex associated to a graph, where the simplices are determined by using certain combinatorial properties of the graph. In recent years, investigating the topological properties of graph complexes has become an increasingly active area of research.
Numerous graph complexes have been introduced and extensively studied, including the neighborhood complex
[
9
,
33
,
38
]
, independence complex
[
4
,
25
,
35
]
, clique complex
[
3
,
26
,
30
]
, and matching complex
[
8
,
31
,
37
]
.
These complexes establish a connection between topology and graph theory. In particular, the combinatorial properties of the underlying graphs can be investigated through topological invariants of these complexes, such as homotopy type, Betti numbers, homology groups, topological connectivity, etc. For further details on graph complexes, we refer the reader to
[
29
,
32
]
.

Recently, Bayer et al.
[
5
]
introduced a new family of graph complexes, called cut complexes. For
k\geq 1
, the
k
-cut complex
of a graph
G
, denoted as
\Delta_{k}(G)
, is the simplicial complex whose facets (maximal simplices) are
\sigma\subseteq V(G)
such that
|\sigma|=|V(G)|-k
(where
|\cdot|
denotes cardinality) and the induced subgraph
G[V(G)\setminus\sigma]
is disconnected.
One of the principal motivations for studying cut complexes arises from a celebrated theorem of Ralf Fröberg connecting commutative algebra and graph theory through topology (see
Theorem

1.1
). For more on connections of graph complexes with commutative algebra, see
[
21
,
36
,
42
]
.

Let
\Delta
be a simplicial complex on the vertex set
V(\Delta)=\{v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{n}\}
and let
\mathbb{K}
be a field. The Stanley-Reisner ideal
I_{\Delta}
of
\Delta
is the ideal of the polynomial ring
\mathbb{K}[x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}]
generated by the monomials corresponding to minimal subsets of
V(\Delta)
, which are not simplices of
\Delta
,
i.e.
,
I_{\Delta}=\langle x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}\ldots x_{i_{k}}:\{v_{i_{1}},v_{i_{2}},\ldots,v_{i_{k}}\}\notin\Delta\rangle
. The Stanley-Reisner ring
\mathbb{K}[\Delta]
is the quotient ring
\mathbb{K}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]/I_{\Delta}
. For more details, we refer the reader to
[
22
]
.

The Alexander dual
\Delta^{\vee}
of the simplicial complex
\Delta
is the simplicial complex on the vertex set
V(\Delta)
, whose simplices are the subsets of
V(\Delta)
such that their complements are not simplices of
\Delta
,
i.e.
,

\Delta^{\vee}=\{\sigma\subset V(\Delta):V(\Delta)\setminus\sigma\notin\Delta\}.

The clique complex
\mathsf{Cl}(G)
of a graph
G
is the simplicial complex whose simplices are
\sigma\subseteq V(G)
such that the induced subgraph
G[\sigma]
is a complete graph. It is easy to check that the Stanley-Reisner ideal
I_{\mathsf{Cl}(G)}
of
\mathsf{Cl}(G)
, is generated by quadratic square-free monomials.

Theorem 1.1

(
[
22
, p. 274]
,
[
23
, Theorem 1]
)
.

A Stanley–Reisner ideal
I_{\Delta}
generated by quadratic square-free monomials has a
2
-linear resolution if and only if
\Delta
is the clique complex
\mathsf{Cl}(G)
of a chordal graph
G
.

The following can be inferred from
[
22
, Proposition 8]
.

Theorem 1.2

(
[
22
]
)
.

G
is chordal if and only if
\mathsf{Cl}(G)^{\vee}
is shellable.

For the definition of a shellable complex, see
Definition

2.2
.
Observe that
\mathsf{Cl}(G)^{\vee}=\Delta_{2}(G)
for any graph
G
. Therefore,
Theorem

1.2
implies the following.

G\ \text{is chordal}\Longleftrightarrow\Delta_{2}(G)\ \text{is shellable}.

The above equivalence shows that
shellability of the
2
-cut complex characterizes chordal graphs.
This connection strongly motivates the systematic study
of higher cut complexes
\Delta_{k}(G)
as natural generalizations of

\Delta_{2}(G)
.

Shellability itself is a well-known concept in topological combinatorics that provides deep insights into the combinatorial structure, topological properties, and algebraic invariants of simplicial complexes. One important consequence is that shellable complexes are homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres.
Shellability has proven useful in many areas, including polytope theory
[
16
,
34
]
, poset theory
[
10
,
11
]
, combinatorial topology
[
27
,
43
]
, topological combinatorics
[
24
,
44
]
, algebraic combinatorics
[
13
,
40
]
, commutative algebra
[
12
,
39
]
, etc. Determining whether a given simplicial complex is shellable is an important and active research direction in topological combinatorics.

The shellability of cut complexes has recently attracted considerable attention.
Bayer et al.
[
5
]
determined shellability criteria for cut complexes of complete bipartite graphs and multipartite graphs, and showed that, for a chordal graph
G
, the 3-cut complex
\Delta_{3}(G)
is shellable.
Subsequent works established shellability results for various structured graph families, including squared path graphs
[
7
]
,
2\times n
grid graphs
[
17
]
and hexagonal grids
[
18
]
.

In this article, we consider the shellability of
3
-cut complexes of
C_{n}^{p}
, the
p
-th power of the cycle graph
C_{n}
on
n
vertices (see
Definition

1.3
for power of graphs).

Definition 1.3
.

For a graph
G
and a positive integer
p
, the
p
-th power graph of
G
is a graph
G^{p}
with
V(G^{p})=V(G)
and
\{u,v\}\in E(G^{p})
if and only if there exists a path between
u
and
v
of length at most
p
in
G
(here, the length of a path is the number of edges in the path). Clearly,
G^{1}=G
.

The graphs
C_{n}^{p}
are also a family of circulant graphs, the Cayley graph of the cyclic group of order
n
(see
Definition

2.1
for the definitions of circulant graphs and Cayley graphs).

Powers of cycles have already been studied in the context of graph complexes. Adamaszek described the homotopy types of independence complexes of
C_{n}^{p}

[
1
]
and also investigated their clique complexes
[
2
]
.
In
[
20
]
, the authors determined the homotopy type of
2
-cut complexes of
C_{n}^{p}
for
n=2p+2
and
n\geq 3p+1
. Further, Bravo
[
15
]
extended the study of
2
-cut complexes to all powers of cycle graphs and proved that
\Delta_{2}(C_{n}^{p})
is homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres. However, the topology of
k
-cut complexes of graph powers remains largely unexplored for
k\geq 3
.

The study of shellability of cut complexes of powers of cycle graphs was initiated by Bayer et al. in
[
5
]
and
[
6
]
. It was shown that the
2
-cut complexes of the cycle graphs
C_{n}
(
[
6
, Theorem 4.15]
) and the squared cycle graphs
C_{n}^{2}
(
[
6
, Proposition 4.19]
) are not shellable.
However,
\Delta_{k}(C_{n})
is shellable for
k\geq 3
(
[
5
, Proposition 7.11]
). Motivated by computational evidence, Bayer et al. conjectured that
\Delta_{k}(C_{n}^{2})
is shellable for
k\geq 3
and
n\geq k+6
(
[
5
, Conjecture 7.25]
). This conjecture was verified for
k=3
in
[
19
]
, where the authors proved that
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{2})
is shellable for
n\geq 9
.

In this article, we extend these results to all powers of cycle graphs for
3
-cut complexes. We prove the following.

Theorem 1.4
.

Let
p\geq 2
and
n\geq 6p-3
. Then
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
is shellable. Moreover,

\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})\simeq\bigvee\limits_{\binom{n-2p}{2}-(2p^{2}+p-1)}\mathbb{S}^{n-4}.

To prove
Theorem

1.4
, we define an order on the facets of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
and show that this order is a shelling order. Then, we characterize and count the number of spanning facets in this shelling order to determine the number of spheres appearing in the wedge in the homotopy type of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
.

This paper is organized as follows:
Section

2
introduces preliminaries from graph theory and simplicial complexes.
Section

3
presents the proof of
Theorem

1.4
; we first define an order on the facets of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
and then proceed in two subsections. In
Section

3.1
, we show that this order is a shelling order. In
Section

3.2
, we determine the spanning facets for the shelling order. Finally,
Section

4
discusses the conclusions and future directions.

2.
Preliminaries

In this section, we recall some basic definitions and results used in this article.

2.1.
Graph

A
graph

G
is a pair
(V(G),E(G))
, where
V(G)
is the set of vertices of
G
and
E(G)\subseteq\binom{V(G)}{2}

denotes the set of edges. For any
u,v\in V(G)
, we say that
u
and
v
are adjacent if
\{u,v\}\in E(G)
. We write
u\sim v
for adjacency and
u\nsim v
for non-adjacency.
A
subgraph

H
of
G
is a graph with
V(H)\subseteq V(G)
and
E(H)\subseteq E(G)
. For a subset
U\subseteq V(G)
, the
induced subgraph

G[U]
is the subgraph with
V(G[U])=U
and
E(G[U])=\{\{a,b\}\in E(G)\ |\ a,b\in U\}
.

For
u,v\in V(G)
, a
path
from
u
to
v
is a sequence of distinct vertices
u=v_{0},v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}=v
such that
v_{i}\sim v_{i+1}
for all
0\leq i\leq n-1
. The
length
of a path is the number of edges in the path.
A graph is
connected
if there exists a path between each pair of its vertices; otherwise, it is
disconnected
. The graph with the empty set
\emptyset
as its set of vertices is considered connected.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
\Gamma
be a group and let
S\subset\Gamma
be a subset not containing the identity element.
The
Cayley graph
of
\Gamma
with respect to
S
is the graph
G(\Gamma,S)
having vertex set
\Gamma
, and
\{u,v\}\in E(G(\Gamma,S))
if and only if
uv^{-1}\in S\cup S^{-1}
, where
S^{-1}=\{x^{-1}\ |\ x\in S\}
.
Let
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
denotes the cyclic group of order
n
. For
n\geq 2
and
S\subset\mathbb{Z}_{n}
such that
0\notin S
, the Cayley graph
G(\mathbb{Z}_{n},S)
is called the
circulant graph
on the generating set
S
.

For
n\geq 3
, let
C_{n}
denote the cycle graph on
n
vertices
\{0,1,\ldots,n-1\}
. Observe that for
p\geq 1
, the
p
-th power
C_{n}^{p}
of the cycle graph is a graph with
V(C_{n}^{p})=V(C_{n})
and
E(C_{n}^{p})=\{\{i,i+j\ (\text{mod $n$})\}\,|\,0\leq i\leq n-1
and
1\leq j\leq p
}.
It is easy to check that for
n\geq 2p+1
,
C_{n}^{p}
is a circulant graph on the generating set
\{1,2,\ldots,p\}
.

We refer the reader to
[
14
]
and
[
41
]
for more details about the graphs.

2.2.
Simplicial complex

A
finite abstract simplicial complex

\Delta
is a collection of finite sets such that if
\tau\in\Delta
and
\sigma\subset\tau
, then
\sigma\in\Delta
.
The elements of
\Delta
are called
simplices
of
\Delta
. If
\sigma\subset\tau
, we say that
\sigma
is a
face
of
\tau
. The
dimension of a simplex

\sigma
is equal to
|\sigma|-1
. The
dimension of an abstract simplicial complex
is the maximum of the dimensions of its simplices.
If a simplex has dimension
d
, it is said to be
d
-
dimensional
. The
0
-dimensional simplices are called
vertices
of
\Delta
. An abstract simplicial complex which is an empty collection of sets is called the
void
abstract simplicial complex, and is denoted by
\emptyset
. The
boundary
of a
d
-dimensional simplex
\sigma
is the simplicial complex, consisting of all faces of
\sigma
of dimension
\leq d-1
and it is denoted by
Bd(\sigma).

A simplex that is not a face of any other simplex is called a
maximal simplex
or
facet
. The set of maximal simplices of
\Delta
is denoted by
M(\Delta)
.
A simplicial complex is called
pure
d
-dimensional
, if all of its maximal simplices are of dimension
d
. A
subcomplex

\Delta^{\prime}
of
\Delta
is a simplicial complex such that
\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}
implies
\sigma\in\Delta
.

In this article, we consider any simplicial complex as a topological space, namely, its geometric realization (see
[
32
]
for details). For terminologies related to algebraic topology, we refer to
[
28
]
.

Definition 2.2

(
[
32
, Definition 12.1]
)
.

A simplicial complex
\Delta
is called
shellable
if its facets can be arranged in a linear order
F_{1},F_{2},\ldots,F_{j}
in such a way that the subcomplex
(\bigcup_{r=1}^{s-1}F_{r})\cap F_{s}
is pure and of dimension
(\dim F_{s}-1)
for all
2\leq s\leq j
. Such an ordering of facets is called a
shelling order
.

In other words, a simplicial complex
\Delta
has a
shelling order

F_{1},F_{2},\ldots,F_{j}
of its facets if and only if for any
r,s
satisfying
1\leq r<s\leq j
, there exists
1\leq t<s
such that
F_{t}\cap F_{s}=F_{s}\setminus\{u\}
for some
u\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
.

A facet
F_{i}

(1<i\leq j)
is called
spanning
with respect to the given shelling order if
Bd(F_{i})\subseteq\bigcup\limits_{l=1}^{i-1}F_{l}
.
It is easy to check that
F_{i}
is a spanning facet if for each
u\in F_{i}
, there exists
1\leq t<i
such that
F_{t}\cap F_{i}=F_{i}\setminus\{u\}
.

The following theorem can be inferred from
[
32
, Theorem 12.3]
.

Theorem 2.3
.

Let
\Delta
be a pure shellable simplicial complex of dimension
d
. Then
\Delta
has the homotopy type of a wedge of
\beta
spheres of dimension
d
, where
\beta
is the number of total spanning facets in a given shelling. Hence
\Delta\simeq\bigvee\limits_{\beta}\mathbb{S}^{d}.

3.
Proof of
Theorem

1.4

To prove
Theorem

1.4
, we construct an order on the facets of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
and show that it is a shelling order. We then identify and count all spanning facets in this shelling order to determine the exact number of spheres in the wedge in the homotopy type of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
. A particularly notable aspect of this order is that, for the
p
-th power of the cycle graph, the set of all facets,
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, is partitioned into exactly
p
ordered subsets.
These subsets are denoted by
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
with
\alpha\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}
, which will be described in detail later. The shelling order is then constructed by arranging the sets
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
such that
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha-1}
precedes
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for all
\alpha\in\{1,2,\ldots,p-1\}
.

Throughout the section, we fix
p\geq 2
and
n\geq 6p-3
. Before proceeding to the main proof, we first introduce some notation and establish important results that will be used later. Define

\mathbbm{c}:=\begin{cases}\frac{n+1}{2}&\text{ if $n$ is odd},\\
\phantom{1}\frac{n}{2}&\text{ if $n$ is even}.\end{cases}

Proposition 3.1
.

We have the following.

(i)

p<3p-1\leq\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2<n-p
.

(ii)

\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}\leq n-2p-1
.

(iii)

Let
\beta\geq 1
. Then
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\beta+1}{2}\leq n-2p-1
. Moreover, if
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\beta+1}{2}=n-2p-1
, then
p=2
. For any integer
z
, if
z\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\beta}{2}
, then
2\mathbbm{c}-z\leq n-2p-1
.

(iv)

\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-2}{2}\leq n-p
.

(v)

\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}\geq p
.

Proof.

(i)

By the definition of
\mathbbm{c}
, we get
\frac{n}{2}\leq\mathbbm{c}\leq\frac{n+1}{2}
. We have
n\geq 6p-3
. If
n=6p-3
(odd), then
\mathbbm{c}=3p-1
; and if
n\geq 6p-2
, then
\mathbbm{c}\geq\frac{n}{2}
implies that
\mathbbm{c}\geq 3p-1
. Hence
\mathbbm{c}\geq 3p-1
.
It follows that
3p-1\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\mathbbm{c}\leq n+1-\mathbbm{c}
, and thus
\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2
. Therefore
3p-1\leq\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2
. Moreover, since
p\geq 2
, we get
p<3p-1
and
n-3p+2<n-p
. Thus
p<3p-1\leq\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2<n-p
.

(ii)

By
(i)
,
\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2
. This implies
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}\leq n-\frac{7p}{2}+2
. Since
p\geq 2
, we get
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}\leq n-2p-1
.

(iii)

We have
\beta\geq 1
. Since
\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2
, it follows that
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\beta+1}{2}\leq n-3p+1.
Using
p\geq 2
, we get
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\beta+1}{2}\leq n-2p-1
.

Suppose
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\beta+1}{2}=n-2p-1
. Then
\mathbbm{c}\leq\frac{n+1}{2}
and
\beta\geq 1
imply
n\leq 4p+1
. Since
n\geq 6p-3
, we get
p\leq 2
. Hence
p=2
(as
p\geq 2
).

Now, suppose
z\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\beta}{2}
. Then
2\mathbbm{c}-z\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\beta}{2}\leq n-2p-\frac{1}{2}
. Since
n
,
p
,
\mathbbm{c}
and
z
are all integers, we obtain
2\mathbbm{c}-z\leq n-2p-1
.

(iv)

Since
\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2
, we get
\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-2}{2}\leq n-\frac{3p-2}{2}=n-p-\frac{p-2}{2}
. Then
p\geq 2
implies that
\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-2}{2}\leq n-p
.

(v)

Since
\mathbbm{c}\geq 3p-1
and
p\geq 2
, it follows that
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}\geq\frac{3p}{2}-1=p+\frac{p}{2}-1\geq p
.

∎

We now define an ordered set
\Omega:=(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\ldots,\omega_{n})
by arranging the elements of
V(C_{n}^{p})=\{0,1,2,\ldots,n-1\}
such that, for all
i\in\{1,2,\ldots,n\}
, the
i
-th element is given by

\omega_{i}=\mathbbm{c}+(-1)^{i-1}\lfloor i/2\rfloor\ (\text{mod}\ n),

where
\lfloor i/2\rfloor
denotes the greatest integer less than or equal to
i/2
.
Observe that

\Omega=\begin{cases}(\mathbbm{c},\mathbbm{c}-1,\mathbbm{c}+1,\mathbbm{c}-2,\mathbbm{c}+2,\dots,n-2,2,n-1,1,0),&\text{ if $n$ is odd},\\
(\mathbbm{c},\mathbbm{c}-1,\mathbbm{c}+1,\mathbbm{c}-2,\mathbbm{c}+2,\dots,2,n-2,1,n-1,0),&\text{ if $n$ is even}.\end{cases}

Using the ordered set
\Omega
, we define an order
<_{\Omega}
on the elements of
V(C_{n}^{p})
as follows: for any
u,v\in V(C_{n}^{p})
, we say that
u<_{\Omega}v
if and only if
u=\omega_{i}
and
v=\omega_{j}
for some
i,j\in\{1,2,\ldots,n\}
with
i<j
.

Remark 3.2

(
[
19
, Remark 3.1]
)
.

Let
u,v\in V(C_{n}^{p})
.

(i)

If
u<\mathbbm{c}
, then
u<_{\Omega}v
if and only if either
v<u
or
v\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-u
.

(ii)

If
u\geq\mathbbm{c}
, then
u<_{\Omega}v
if and only if either
v<2\mathbbm{c}-u
or
v>u
.

(iii)

If
v<\mathbbm{c}
, then
u<_{\Omega}v
if and only if
v<u<2\mathbbm{c}-v
.

(iv)

If
v>\mathbbm{c}
, then
u<_{\Omega}v
if and only if
2\mathbbm{c}-v\leq u<v
.

The complement of a set
X\subseteq V(C_{n}^{p})
is denoted by
X^{c}
throughout the proof. For a positive integer
m
, let
[m]=\{1,2,\ldots,m\}
and
[0,m]=[m]\cup\{0\}
.

Let
\mathcal{A}=\{A\subseteq V(C_{n}^{p}):|A|=n-3\}
. For
i\in[n-2]
, define the subsets
\mathcal{A}_{i}
of
\mathcal{A}
as follows:

(1)

\mathcal{A}_{i}:=\begin{cases}\{A\in\mathcal{A}:\omega_{1}\in A^{c}\},&\text{if $i=1$,}\\
\{A\in\mathcal{A}:\omega_{i}\in A^{c}\ \text{and }\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\dots,\omega_{i-1}\notin A^{c}\},&\text{if $i>1$.}\end{cases}

Observe that the sets
\mathcal{A}_{i}
are pairwise disjoint and form a partition of
\mathcal{A}
. So
\mathcal{A}=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{n-2}\mathcal{A}_{i}
.

Let
A\in\mathcal{A}
such that
A\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
for some
i\in[n-2]
. By the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{i}
, we have
\omega_{i}\in A^{c}
. It follows that
A^{c}=\{\omega_{i},i_{1},i_{2}\}
for some
i_{1},i_{2}\in V(C_{n}^{p})
.
Throughout this section, we assume that if
A\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
A^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
, then
i_{1}<i_{2}
.

Remark 3.3

(
[
19
, Remark 3.2]
)
.

Let
A\in\mathcal{A}
such that
A\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
A^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Then
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1},i_{2}
by the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{i}
.

We know that each
A\in\mathcal{A}
is uniquely associated with subset
\mathcal{A}_{i}
for some
i
. Using this partitioning of
\mathcal{A}
into subsets
\mathcal{A}_{i}
, we define an order
\ll
on the elements of
\mathcal{A}
. The following definition precisely formulates this order.

Definition 3.4
.

Define an order
\ll
on the elements of
\mathcal{A}
as follows: for
A,A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
, let
A\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{j}
such that
A^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
and
A^{\prime c}=\{\omega_{j}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
. Then
A\ll A^{\prime}
if and only if any one of the following conditions is true:

(i)

i=j
,
i.e.
,
\omega_{i}=\omega_{j}
, and either
i_{1}<j_{1}
or,
i_{1}=j_{1}
and
i_{2}<j_{2}
.

(ii)

i<j
,
i.e.
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}\omega_{j}
.

It is easy to see that
Definition

3.4
defines a total order on
\mathcal{A}
.

The set of all the facets of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
is denoted by
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. By the definition of the
3
-cut complex, for any
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, we have
|F|=n-3
and the induced subgraph
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected. Clearly,
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))\subset\mathcal{A}=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{n-2}\mathcal{A}_{i}
.
Therefore, the order
\ll
defined on
\mathcal{A}
can be naturally restricted to
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, resulting in a total order on the elements of
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

To obtain the desired shelling order, we modify the order
\ll
. This is done by partitioning
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
into
p
disjoint sets
\mathcal{M}_{0},\mathcal{M}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{p-1}
.
For
\alpha\in[p-1]
, define the subsets
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
(the subset
\mathcal{M}_{0}
is defined later) of
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
as follows:
a facet
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
if and only if
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
for some
i\in[n-2]
such that
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
, and
F
satisfies one of the following conditions:

(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})

i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}
with
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1
.

(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})

i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}
with
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{1}=\omega_{i}-2p+\alpha-1
.

(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})

i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
with
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
.

(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})

i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
with
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
,
\omega_{i}-p+\alpha\leq i_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p-1
and
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
.

(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})

i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
with
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
,
i_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\alpha
and
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\alpha
(
\implies

i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p
).

(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})

i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
with
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1
and
i_{2}\leq i_{1}+2p-\alpha
(
\implies

i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-1
).

(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})

i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
with
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
,
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
and
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p\leq i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p-\alpha
.

(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})

i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
with
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
.

(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})

\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
with
\omega_{i}\leq n-2p-2
,
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
and
i_{2}=\omega_{i}+2p-\alpha+1
.

(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})

\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
with
\omega_{i}>n-2p-2
,
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
and
i_{2}=n-\alpha-1
.

We now show that the sets
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
, where
\alpha\in[p-1]
, are pairwise disjoint. To do so, we first derive a few preliminary results.
Recall that for any
u,v\in V(C_{n}^{p})
, the notation
u\sim v
denotes that the vertices
u
and
v
are adjacent in
C_{n}^{p}
. This adjacency holds if and only if

v=u\pm j\ (\text{mod $n$})\text{ for some }j\in[p].

If
u
and
v
are not adjacent, then we write
u\nsim v
.

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. If
i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}
and
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p
, then
i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}-p-1
.

Proof.

Since
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
,
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected. Suppose
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}
. Then
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}\geq\omega_{i}
, which implies
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}-p
. Hence
\omega_{i}-p\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}
. This means that
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is connected, a contradiction. Therefore
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}
.
By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
. Since
i_{2}<w_{i}
,
Remark

3.2
(ii)
gives
i_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. Then
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p\leq i_{1}<i_{2}
implies
i_{2}-p<i_{1}<i_{2}
. Hence
i_{1}\sim i_{2}
. Using the fact that
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected, it follows that
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
, and therefore
i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}-p-1
.
∎

The following result provides additional information about a facet
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
.

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Assume
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
.

(i)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
, then
\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha+1}{2}\leq\omega_{i}<n-p
.

(ii)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
, then
\omega_{i}<n-p
.

(iii)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
, then
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-2p+\alpha-1
,
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}-p+\alpha
,
i_{1}>p
,
i_{2}<n-p
and
i_{1}\sim\omega_{i}
.

(iv)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
, then
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
,
i_{1}>p
,
i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\alpha
,
i_{2}\leq n-p
and
i_{1}\sim\omega_{i}
.

(v)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
, then
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p-1}{2}
,
i_{1}>p
,
i_{2}<n-p
and
i_{1}\sim\omega_{i}
.

(vi)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
, then
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p-2}{2}
,
i_{1}>p
,
i_{2}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha}{2}+p-1\leq\omega_{i}+p-\alpha-1
,
i_{2}<n-p
and
i_{2}\sim\omega_{i}
.

(vii)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
, then
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1
,
i_{1}>p
,
i_{2}<n-p
and
i_{2}\sim\omega_{i}
.

(viii)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
, then
i_{1}>p
,
i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p-\alpha<\omega_{i}+p
,
i_{2}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p}{2}-1
,
i_{2}\leq n-p
and
i_{2}\sim\omega_{i}
.

(ix)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
, then
\omega_{i}>2p
and
i_{1}\sim i_{2}
.

(x)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
, then
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{i}\geq 2p
and
i_{1}\sim i_{2}
.

Proof.

By
Remark

3.3
, we have
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1},i_{2}
.

(i)

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
. Then
i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1
. By
Proposition

3.5
,
i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}-p-1
.
It follows that
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1<\omega_{i}-p-1
, which implies
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
. Since
p\geq 2
, we have
\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-2}{2}
. Moreover,
\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-2}{2}\leq n-p
by
Proposition

3.1
(iv)
. Thus,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
implies
\omega_{i}<n-p
.

(ii)

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
. Then
i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}
,
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{1}=\omega_{i}-2p+\alpha-1
. Note that
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}
.
Since
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
and
i_{2}<\omega_{i}
, we have
i_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
. This gives
\omega_{i}-2p+\alpha-1=i_{1}<i_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
, which implies
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{2p-\alpha-1}{2}
.
Since
\alpha\geq 1
and
p\geq 2
, it follows that
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{2p-2}{2}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-2}{2}
. Hence
\omega_{i}<n-p
by
Proposition

3.1
(iv)
.

(iii)

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
. Then
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
. Clearly,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
. By
Remark

3.2
(i)
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
and
i_{2}>\omega_{i}
yield
i_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
.
Since
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
, this implies
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-2p+\alpha-1
. Using
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
, we get
i_{1}>\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}+\alpha-1>\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1\geq\omega_{i}-p
(as
\alpha\geq 1
). This means that
\omega_{i}-p<i_{1}<\omega_{i}
, and hence
i_{1}\sim\omega_{i}
.
Moreover,
i_{1}>\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}+\alpha-1\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}
. Since
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}\geq p
by
Proposition

3.1
(v)
, we obtain
i_{1}>p
.
We have
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\alpha+1\leq i_{1}+2p
and
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
. It follows that
i_{2}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-2}{2}
. Thus,
i_{2}<n-p
by
Proposition

3.1
(iv)
.

(iv)

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
. Then
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
,
\omega_{i}-p+\alpha\leq i_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p-1
and
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
.
Using
\omega_{i}-p+\alpha\leq i_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p-1
, we obtain
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
. Note that
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}-p+\alpha
imply
i_{1}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}+\alpha>\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}
.
Therefore
i_{1}>p
by
Proposition

3.1
(v)
. Since
i_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p-1
and
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
, we get
i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\alpha\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-1
. Further,
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
implies
i_{2}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-2}{2}
.
Hence
i_{2}\leq n-p
by
Proposition

3.1
(iv)
. Finally,
\omega_{i}-p<\omega_{i}-p+\alpha\leq i_{1}<\omega_{i}
implies
i_{1}\sim\omega_{i}
.

(v)

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
,
i_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\alpha
and
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p
. Since
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
, we have
\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
.
From
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p\leq i_{1}<\omega_{i}
, we obtain
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p-1}{2}
and
\omega_{i}-p<i_{1}<\omega_{i}
. It follows that
i_{1}\sim\omega_{i}
and
i_{1}>\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p-1}{2}
. By
Proposition

3.1
(v)
,
i_{1}>p
.
Moreover,
i_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\alpha
and
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p-1}{2}
imply
i_{2}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-1}{2}-\alpha\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-3}{2}
. Thus
i_{2}<n-p
by
Proposition

3.1
(iv)
.

(vi)

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
. Then
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1
and
i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-1
.
Using
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
and
i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-1
, we obtain
i_{2}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha}{2}+p-1\leq\omega_{i}+p-\alpha-1
.
Note that
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}
implies
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}<\omega_{i}
. Since
\omega_{i}<i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-1
, we have
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p-2}{2}
and
\omega_{i}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}+p
. This gives
i_{2}\sim\omega_{i}
and
i_{2}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-4}{2}
. Hence
i_{2}<n-p
by
Proposition

3.1
(iv)
.
Now, since
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1
and
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p-2}{2}
, we get
i_{1}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p-2}{2}+\alpha-1\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p-2}{2}
. By
Proposition

3.1
(v)
,
i_{1}>p
.

(vii)

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
. Then
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
,
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
and
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p\leq i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p-\alpha
. From the bounds on
i_{2}
, we obtain
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
.
Moreover,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p-\alpha
imply
i_{2}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p}{2}-\alpha\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-2}{2}
. Therefore
i_{2}<n-p
by
Proposition

3.1
(iv)
.
Since
i_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p
and
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
, we get
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p
. Further,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
implies
i_{1}>\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}
. By
Proposition

3.1
(v)
,
i_{1}>p
. Now,
\omega_{i}<i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p-\alpha<\omega_{i}+p
implies
i_{2}\sim\omega_{i}
.

(viii)

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. Then
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
. Clearly,
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}
. Since
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
and
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
,
Remark

3.2
(ii)
implies
i_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. Using
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
, we obtain
i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+2p-\alpha
.
Further,
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
gives
i_{2}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p}{2}-\alpha\leq\omega_{i}+p-\alpha<\omega_{i}+p
. Since
\omega_{i}<i_{2}
, we get
i_{2}\sim\omega_{i}
.
Moreover,
i_{2}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p}{2}-\alpha\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p-2}{2}
. By
Proposition

3.1
(iv)
,
i_{2}\leq n-p
.
Now,
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\alpha-1\geq i_{2}-2p
and
\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}\leq\omega_{i}<i_{2}
imply
i_{1}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}+1
. Hence
i_{1}>p
by
Proposition

3.1
(v)
.

(ix)

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
. Then
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
,
\omega_{i}\leq n-2p-2
,
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
and
i_{2}=\omega_{i}+2p-\alpha+1
. Since
i_{1}<i_{2}=\omega_{i}+2p-\alpha+1\leq\omega_{i}+2p<i_{1}+p
, we have
i_{1}\sim i_{2}
. By
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
\mathbbm{c}\geq 3p-1
. If
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}
, then
\omega_{i}>2p
(as
p\geq 2
). Otherwise, if
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
, then
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
and
i_{1}>\omega_{i}
imply
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
. Since
i_{1}<i_{2}=\omega_{i}+2p-\alpha+1
, it follows that
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{2p-\alpha}{2}\geq 3p-1-\frac{2p-1}{2}>2p
. Hence, in either case,
\omega_{i}>2p
.

(x)

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
. Then
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
,
\omega_{i}>n-2p-2
,
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
and
i_{2}=n-\alpha-1
. Since
n\geq 6p-3
and
p\geq 2
, it follows that
\omega_{i}\geq n-2p-1\geq 4p-4\geq 2p
. Note that
i_{1}<i_{2}=n-\alpha-1<\omega_{i}+2p-\alpha+1\leq\omega_{i}+2p<i_{1}+p
. Hence
i_{1}\sim i_{2}
.
Moreover,
n-p\leq\omega_{i}+p+1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}=n-\alpha-1\leq n-2
. This implies
p\geq 3
. Now, suppose
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
. Then
n-2p-1\leq\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}\leq\frac{n+1}{2}
, which yields
n<4p+3
. Since
n\geq 6p-3
, we get
p<3
, a contradiction. Therefore
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}
.

∎

Remark 3.7
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Suppose
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
for some
\beta\in[p-1]
.

(i)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{5})
, then
i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\beta
.

(ii)

If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
, then
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta-1
.

Proposition 3.8
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
with
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
and
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}
.
Suppose
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
for some
\beta\in[p-1]
. Then
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{8})
. Moreover, if
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
.

Proof.

Since
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
for some
\beta\in[p-1]
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
with
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
F
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{8})
. If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{5})
, then
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
; and if
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{4})
, then
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
by
Proposition

3.6
(iv)
. Therefore,
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}
implies that
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{8})
.
Moreover, if
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
F
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{8})
, so
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
.
∎

Proposition 3.9
.

For any distinct
\alpha,\beta\in[p-1]
,
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}\cap\mathcal{M}_{\beta}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

On the contrary, suppose that
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}\cap\mathcal{M}_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
. Then there exists a facet
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}\cap\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. Since
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
, it follows that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
such that
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
, and
F
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
.

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
. Then
i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}
,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1
.
Therefore,
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{1})
. It follows that
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta-1
, thereby implying that
\alpha=\beta
, a contradiction.
Now, if
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
, then a similar argument leads to a contradiction to
\alpha\neq\beta
.

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
. Then
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
.
Therefore,
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{5})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
. If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{3})
, then
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\beta+1
, which implies
\alpha=\beta
, a contradiction. Now, if
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{5})
, then
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p-1}{2}
by
Proposition

3.6
(v)
; and if
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
, then
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\beta}{2}
by
Proposition

3.6
(vii)
. Both contradict
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
.

Suppose
F
satisfies any of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. Then, using
Proposition

3.6
and a similar argument as above, we again get a contradiction to
\alpha\neq\beta
.

Hence our assumption that
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}\cap\mathcal{M}_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
is false.
∎

Define the subset
\mathcal{M}_{0}
of
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
as

\mathcal{M}_{0}:=M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))\setminus(\sqcup_{\alpha=1}^{p-1}\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}).

Using
Proposition

3.9
, we conclude that
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))=\bigsqcup_{\alpha=0}^{p-1}\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
.

We now define an order
\prec
on the elements of
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, which we prove to be a shelling order for the facets of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
. For this, we modify the order
\ll
by repositioning the elements of the sets
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in[p-1]
in the poset
(M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})),\ll)
, leading to a new poset
(M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})),\prec)
.

Definition 3.10
.

The order
\prec
on the elements of
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
is defined as follows. Let
F,F^{\prime}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Then
F\prec F^{\prime}
if and only if any one of the following conditions is true:

(i)

F\ll F^{\prime}
and
F,F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[0,p-1]
.

(ii)

F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
for some
\alpha,\beta\in[0,p-1]
and
\alpha<\beta
.

Observe that the order
\prec
on the elements of
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
is defined such that
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha-1}
precedes
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for all
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Moreover, within each
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
, where
\alpha\in[0,p-1]
, all facets are ordered using the order
\ll
. Hence,
\prec
is a total order.
To show that
\prec
provides a shelling order for the facets of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
, we first prove several key results.

Proposition 3.11
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
.

(i)

If
\omega_{i}-p\leq i_{1}<\omega_{i}
, then
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
and
i_{2}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1>\omega_{i}
.

(ii)

If
\omega_{i}<i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p
, then
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
and
i_{1}\leq\omega_{i}-p-1<\omega_{i}
.

(iii)

If
i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}
, then
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
.

(iv)

If
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
, then
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
.

Proof.

Since
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
,
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected. Recall that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
implies
i_{1}<i_{2}
by our assumption.

(i)

Since
\omega_{i}-p\leq i_{1}<\omega_{i}
, we have
i_{1}\sim\omega_{i}
. The fact that
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected implies
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
. Using
i_{1}<i_{2}
, we obtain
i_{2}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1>\omega_{i}
.

(ii)

Since
\omega_{i}<i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p
, we get
i_{2}\sim\omega_{i}
. Then,
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected implies
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
. Moreover,
i_{1}<i_{2}
gives
i_{1}\leq\omega_{i}-p-1<\omega_{i}
.

(iii)

We have
0\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}\leq n-1
. Suppose
i_{1}\sim\omega_{i}
. Since
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected, we get
i_{1}\nsim i_{2}
, and hence
i_{1}\leq\omega_{i}+p\ (\text{mod }n)
. This means that
\omega_{i}\geq n-p
. Since
n-p>\mathbbm{c}
by
Proposition

3.1
(i)
, we get
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}
.
By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
. Therefore
i_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
. Using
\omega_{i}\geq n-p
, we obtain
i_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-n+p\leq p+1
(as
2\mathbbm{c}\in\{n,n+1\}
). It follows that
0\leq i_{1}<i_{2}\leq p
, which implies
i_{1}\sim i_{2}
, a contradiction. Hence
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
.

(iv)

We have
0\leq\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}\leq n-1
. Suppose
i_{2}\sim\omega_{i}
. Using the fact that
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected, we get
i_{1}\nsim i_{2}
, and therefore
\omega_{i}\leq i_{2}+p\ (\text{mod }n)
. This means that
\omega_{i}\leq p-1
. Since
p<\mathbbm{c}
by
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
.
By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
, and hence by
Remark

3.2
(i)
,
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. Now,
\omega_{i}\leq p-1
implies
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-p+1\geq n-p+1
. This gives
n-p+1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}\leq n-1
, and thus
i_{1}\sim i_{2}
, a contradiction. Hence
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
.

∎

Proposition 3.12
.

Let
F,F^{\prime}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and let
\beta\in[p-1]
. Assume that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
such that
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
.

(1)

Suppose
i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}
and
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.

(i)

If
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta-1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(2)

Suppose
i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}
and
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.

(i)

If
i_{1}=\omega_{i}-2p+\beta-1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}-2p+\beta
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(3)

Suppose
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
and
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
.

(i)

If
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\beta+1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
i_{2}\leq i_{1}+2p-\beta
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(4)

Suppose
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
,
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
and
\omega_{i}-p\leq i_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p-1
.

(i)

If
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\beta+1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
i_{2}\leq i_{1}+2p-\beta
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(5)

Suppose
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
,
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
,
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}-p
and
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p
.

(i)

If
i_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\beta
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\beta-1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(6)

Suppose
\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
,
i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p
and
i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-1
.

(i)

If
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta-1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(7)

Suppose
\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p\leq i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p
.

(i)

If
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\beta
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(8)

Suppose
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
and
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.

(i)

If
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\beta
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(9)

Suppose
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
and
\omega_{i}\leq n-2p-2
. Further, assume that
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
or
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-p
.

(i)

If
i_{2}=\omega_{i}+2p-\beta+1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+2p-\beta
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(10)

Suppose
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
and
\omega_{i}>n-2p-2
. Further, assume that
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
or
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-p
.

(i)

If
i_{2}=n-\beta-1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
i_{2}\leq n-\beta-2
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

Proof.

(1)

If
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta-1
, then
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{1})
, and therefore
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
.

Now, let
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta
. Then
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta+\gamma-1
for some
\gamma\geq 1
. By
Proposition

3.5
,
i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}-p-1
. Using
i_{1}<i_{2}
, we get
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2}
.
Since
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
,
\beta+\gamma<p-1
. Moreover,
\beta+\gamma>\beta\geq 1
. This gives
\beta+\gamma\in[p-1]
. Therefore
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta+\gamma}^{1})
, and thus
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta+\gamma}
.
Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(2)

If
i_{1}=\omega_{i}-2p+\beta-1
, then
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{2})
, and thus
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
.

Now, let
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}-2p+\beta
. Then
i_{1}=\omega_{i}-2p+\beta+\gamma-1
for some
\gamma\geq 1
. By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
. Since
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}
and
i_{2}<\omega_{i}
,
Remark

3.2
(ii)
gives
i_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. Using
i_{1}<i_{2}
, we obtain
i_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-2
, which implies
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{2p-\beta-\gamma-1}{2}
. Since
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, we have
\beta+\gamma\leq p-1
.
Therefore
\beta+\gamma>\beta\geq 1
implies
\beta+\gamma\in[p-1]
. Note that
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta+\gamma}^{2})
, and thus
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta+\gamma}
.
Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(3)

If
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\beta+1
, then
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{3})
, and therefore
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
.

Now, let
i_{2}\leq i_{1}+2p-\beta
. Then
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\beta-\gamma+1
for some
\gamma\geq 1
. By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
. Since
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
and
i_{2}>\omega_{i}
, we have
i_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}

Remark

3.2
(i)
. This implies
\omega_{i}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-i_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-i_{1}-2p+\beta+\gamma-1
. Using
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
, we obtain
\omega_{i}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-2p+\beta+\gamma
, and hence
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{2p-\beta-\gamma}{2}
.
Further,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
implies
\beta+\gamma\leq p-1
. Since
\beta+\gamma>\beta\geq 1
, we get
\beta+\gamma\in[p-1]
. Therefore
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta+\gamma}^{3})
, and thus
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta+\gamma}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(4)

Since
\omega_{i}-p\leq i_{1}<\omega_{i}
, we get
i_{2}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1>\omega_{i}
by
Proposition

3.11
(i)
. Hence
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
and
i_{2}\geq i_{1}+p+2
.

If
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\beta+1
, then
i_{2}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
implies
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}-p+\beta
. This means that
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{4})
, and therefore
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
.

Now, let
i_{2}\leq i_{1}+2p-\beta
. Then
i_{2}\geq i_{1}+p+2
implies
i_{2}=i_{1}+2p-\beta-\gamma+1
for some
1\leq\gamma\leq p-\beta-1
. Note that
\beta+\gamma\leq p-1
. Since
\beta+\gamma>\beta\geq 1
, we have
\beta+\gamma\in[p-1]
. Moreover,
i_{2}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
implies
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}-p+\beta+\gamma
.
Therefore
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta+\gamma}^{4})
, and thus
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta+\gamma}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(5)

Since
\omega_{i}-p\leq i_{1}<\omega_{i}
,
Proposition

3.11
(i)
gives
i_{2}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1>\omega_{i}
. Hence
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
.

If
i_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\beta
, then
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\beta\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
, which implies
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\beta+1}{2}
.
Observe that
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{5})
, and hence
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. Therefore
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
.

Now, let
i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\beta-1
. Then
i_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\beta-\gamma
for some
\gamma\geq 1
. Therefore,
i_{2}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
implies
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\beta+\gamma+1}{2}
. Since
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
, we get
\beta+\gamma\leq p-1
. Moreover,
\beta+\gamma>\beta\geq 1
. It follows that
\beta+\gamma\in[p-1]
.
Thus
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta+\gamma}^{5})
, which implies
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta+\gamma}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(6)

By
Proposition

3.11
(ii)
,
\omega_{i}<i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p
implies
i_{1}\leq\omega_{i}-p-1<\omega_{i}
. Hence
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
.

If
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta-1
, then
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta-1\leq\omega_{i}-p-1
, which implies
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\beta}{2}
.
Therefore
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{6})
, and hence
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. Thus
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
.

Now, let
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta
. Then
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta+\gamma-1
for some
\gamma\geq 1
. Using
i_{1}\leq\omega_{i}-p-1
, we obtain
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2}
. Further,
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
implies
\beta+\gamma\leq p-1
. Since
\beta+\gamma>\beta\geq 1
, it follows that
\beta+\gamma\in[p-1]
.
Therefore
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta+\gamma}^{6})
, and thus
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta+\gamma}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(7)

Since
\omega_{i}<i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p
,
Proposition

3.11
(ii)
gives
i_{1}\leq\omega_{i}-p-1<\omega_{i}
. Hence
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
and
i_{1}\leq i_{2}-p-2
.

If
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
, then
i_{1}\leq\omega_{i}-p-1
implies
i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p-\beta
. Therefore
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
, and hence
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. Thus
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
.

Now, let
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\beta
. Then
i_{1}\leq i_{2}-p-2
implies
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\beta+\gamma-1
for some
1\leq\gamma\leq p-\beta-1
. Clearly,
\beta+\gamma\leq p-1
. Since
\beta+\gamma>\beta\geq 1
, we have
\beta+\gamma\in[p-1]
. Moreover,
i_{1}\leq\omega_{i}-p-1
implies
i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p-\beta-\gamma
.
Therefore
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta+\gamma}^{7})
, and thus
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta+\gamma}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(8)

If
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
, then
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{8})
and therefore
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
.

Now, let
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\beta
. Then
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\beta+\gamma-1
for some
\gamma\geq 1
. By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
. Therefore,
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}
and
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
imply
i_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
. It follows that
\omega_{i}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-i_{1}-1=2\mathbbm{c}-i_{2}+2p-\beta-\gamma
.
Since
i_{2}>\omega_{i}
, we get
\omega_{i}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+2p-\beta-\gamma-1
, and thus
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{2p-\beta-\gamma-1}{2}
. Using
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, we obtain
\beta+\gamma\leq p-1
. Moreover,
\beta+\gamma>\beta\geq 1
, which implies
\beta+\gamma\in[p-1]
.
Observe that
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta+\gamma}^{8})
, and thus
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta+\gamma}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(9)

We have
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
and
\omega_{i}\leq n-2p-2
. Furthermore,
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
or
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-p
.

(a)

Let
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
. If
i_{2}=\omega_{i}+2p-\beta+1
, then
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{9})
and thus
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
(as
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
).

Now, suppose
i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+2p-\beta
. Since
i_{2}>i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
,
i_{2}=\omega_{i}+2p-\beta-\gamma+1
for some
1\leq\gamma\leq p-\beta-1
. Note that
\beta+\gamma\in[p-1]
.
Therefore
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta+\gamma}^{9})
, and hence
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta+\gamma}
. Thus
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(b)

Let
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-p
. Then
i_{1}<i_{2}
implies
i_{1}\sim i_{2}
. Since
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
,
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected. Hence
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
, and thus
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
(as
i_{1}>\omega_{i}
). Therefore, the result follows from case (a).

(10)

We have
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
and
\omega_{i}>n-2p-2
. Furthermore,
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
or
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-p
.

(a)

Let
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
. If
i_{2}=n-\beta-1
, then
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{10})
and therefore
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. Hence
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
(as
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
).

Now, assume
i_{2}\leq n-\beta-2
. Since
\omega_{i}>n-2p-2
and
i_{2}>i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
, we get
i_{2}>n-p
. This means that
i_{2}=n-\beta-\gamma-1
for some
1\leq\gamma<p-\beta-1
.
Clearly,
\beta+\gamma\in[p-1]
. Therefore
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta+\gamma}^{10})
, and hence
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta+\gamma}
. Thus
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
implies
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(b)

Let
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-p
. Since
i_{1}<i_{2}
, we have
i_{1}\sim i_{2}
. Then,
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
implies
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
. Hence
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
(as
i_{1}>\omega_{i}
). Therefore, the result follows from case (a).

∎

Corollary 3.13
.

Let
F,F^{\prime}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, and let
\beta\in[p-1]
. Assume
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
with
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
.

(i)

If
\omega_{i}-p\leq i_{1}<\omega_{i}
,
i_{2}\leq i_{1}+2p-\beta+1
and
i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\beta
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
\omega_{i}<i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+p
,
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
and
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta-1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

Proof.

(i)

By
Proposition

3.11
(i)
, we have
i_{2}>\omega_{i}
. Observe that if
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(3)
.
Now, assume that
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
. If
i_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p-1
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(4)
, and if
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p
, then
F\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(5)
.
Similarly, using
Proposition

3.12
(3)
,
(4)
and
(5)
, if
F^{\prime}\ll F
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
.

(ii)

By
Proposition

3.11
(ii)
, we have
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
. The proof follows a similar argument as in
(i)
, using
Proposition

3.12
(6)
,
(7)
and
(8)
.

∎

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. For
u\in F
and
v\in F^{c}
, we introduce the notation
{F({u},{v})}
to represent the set obtained by replacing
u
in
F
with
v
, formally defined as

{F({u},{v})}:=(F\setminus\{u\})\sqcup\{v\}.

It is easy to see that
{F({u},{v})}\cap F=F\setminus\{u\}
and
({F({u},{v})})^{c}=(F^{c}\setminus\{v\})\sqcup\{u\}
. These relations will be used later in our arguments.

3.1.
Shelling Order

This section focuses on proving that the order
\prec
, defined on the facets of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
is a shelling order. We begin by introducing several results that will be used in the proof.

Proposition 3.14
.

Let
F,F^{\prime}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F,F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
. Suppose
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
and
F^{\prime c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
.
If
i_{1}\in F^{\prime}
,
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<j_{2}
with
j_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
, and
F^{\prime}(i_{1},j_{1})\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, then
\omega_{i}\leq 2p-1
,
\omega_{i}<i_{2}<j_{2}
,
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
and
i_{1}\nsim i_{2}
.

Proof.

We have
(F^{\prime}(i_{1},j_{1}))^{c}=(F^{\prime c}\setminus\{j_{1}\})\sqcup\{i_{1}\}=\{\omega_{i},j_{2},i_{1}\}
. Since
F^{\prime}(i_{1},j_{1})\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, we get
C_{n}^{p}[(F^{\prime}(i_{1},j_{1}))^{c}]
is connected. Therefore,
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<j_{2}
with
j_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
implies
j_{2}\sim i_{1}
and
i_{1}\sim\omega_{i}
.
Hence
\omega_{i}\leq j_{2}+2p\ (\text{mod }n)\leq 2p-1
(as
j_{2}\leq n-1
). Moreover,
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
implies
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected. Since
i_{1}\sim\omega_{i}
, we have
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
and
i_{1}\nsim i_{2}
.
Using
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<j_{2}
,
j_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
,
j_{2}\sim i_{1}
,
i_{1}\sim\omega_{i}
and
i_{1}\nsim i_{2}
, we obtain
\omega_{i}<i_{2}<j_{2}
.
∎

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. If
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
and
\omega_{i}\leq 2p-1
, then
F\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Since
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
, it follows that
F
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. By
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
\mathbbm{c}\geq 3p-1
. Hence,
\omega_{i}\leq 2p-1
implies
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-p<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
.
This means that
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
. If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
, then
Proposition

3.6
(v)
and
(vii)
contradict
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
.
Therefore
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
. By
Proposition

3.6
(iii)
,
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-2p+\alpha-1
. Since
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-p
and
\alpha\geq 1
, it follows that
i_{1}\geq\mathbbm{c}-p\geq\omega_{i}
, a contradiction. Hence
F\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
∎

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
F,F^{\prime}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and let
\beta\in[p-1]
. Assume
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
with
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
, and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
. If
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
2\mathbbm{c}-i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-i_{2}-p+\beta-1
,
i_{2}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
F\ll F^{\prime}
, then
F\prec F^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We show that
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
0\leq\alpha\leq\beta
. Then, since
F\ll F^{\prime}
, it follows that
F\prec F^{\prime}
by
Definition

3.10
. Suppose that
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\beta<\alpha\leq p-1
. We have
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
and
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}<i_{2}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
. Thus,
F
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
.

Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
. Then
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}-p+\alpha
. Since
\omega_{i}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-i_{2}
and
\alpha>\beta
, it follows that
i_{1}>2\mathbbm{c}-i_{2}-p+\beta
, a contradiction. Hence
F
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
.
Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
and
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p
. We get
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p\leq i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-i_{2}-p+\beta-1<\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1
, which implies that
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha-1}{2}
, a contradiction. Hence
F
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
.
Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
. Then
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1
. Since
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-i_{2}-p+\beta-1
and
\alpha>\beta
, we get
\omega_{i}=i_{2}+\alpha-\beta>i_{2}
, a contradiction as
\omega_{i}<i_{2}
. Hence
F
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
.
Suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
. Then
i_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p
. Since
\omega_{i}<i_{2}
and
i_{2}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, it follows that
i_{2}>2\mathbbm{c}-i_{2}+p>\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, a contradiction. Hence
F
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
.

Therefore
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
0\leq\alpha\leq\beta
.
∎

Proposition 3.17
.

Let
F,F^{\prime}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Let
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{j}
such that
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
and
F^{\prime c}=\{\omega_{j}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
with
j_{1}<\omega_{j}<i_{2}
,
j_{1}\leq i_{1}<i_{2}
and
F\prec F^{\prime}
.
Assume that if
\omega_{i}=\omega_{j}
, then
j_{1}<i_{1}
.
Let
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
for some
\beta\in[p-1]
, where
\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\beta}{2}\leq\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
j_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p+\beta-1
.
Suppose
i_{2}\in F^{\prime}
,
(F^{\prime}(i_{2},j_{2}))^{c}=\{\omega_{j}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},i_{2}\}
and
F^{\prime}(i_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{\gamma}
for some
\beta\leq\gamma\leq p-1
. Then
i_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}+p
and
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}\leq\omega_{i}<\omega_{j}
.

Proof.

We have
(F^{\prime}(i_{2},j_{2}))^{c}=\{\omega_{j}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},i_{2}\}
and
F^{\prime}(i_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{\gamma}
for some
1\leq\beta\leq\gamma\leq p-1
. Since
j_{1}<\omega_{j}<i_{2}
and
\mathbbm{c}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\beta}{2}\leq\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
,
F^{\prime}(i_{2},j_{2})
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}^{7})
by
Proposition

3.8
.

If
F^{\prime}(i_{2},j_{2})
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}^{6})
, then
j_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p+\gamma-1
and
j_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\gamma
, and by
Proposition

3.6
(vi)
,
i_{2}\leq\omega_{j}+p-\gamma-1
. Moreover, if
F^{\prime}(i_{2},j_{2})
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}^{7})
, then
j_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\gamma-1
and
i_{2}\leq\omega_{j}+p-\gamma
, and by
Proposition

3.6
(vii)
,
j_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p+\gamma-1
.
Therefore, in both cases, we have
j_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p+\gamma-1
,
j_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\gamma-1
, and
i_{2}\leq\omega_{j}+p-\gamma
.
Further, using
j_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p+\beta-1
and
\gamma\geq\beta
, we obtain
\gamma=\beta
. Thus
j_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
and
i_{2}\leq\omega_{j}+p-\beta
. Note that
i_{2}\leq j_{1}+2p-\beta+1=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}+p
. Since
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
, we get
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}<\omega_{j}
.

We first show that
\omega_{i}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
. Suppose that
\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
. Since
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}\omega_{i}
by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
.
Hence
F^{\prime}\ll F
by
Definition

3.4
(ii)
. We have
i_{1}\geq j_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
,
i_{1}\geq j_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p+\beta-1>\omega_{i}-p+\beta-1\geq\omega_{i}-p
and
i_{2}\leq\omega_{j}+p-\beta<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}+p-\beta
.
Therefore, if
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
, then
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Corollary

3.13
(i)
, a contradiction to our assumption that
F\prec F^{\prime}
. Hence
i_{1}>\omega_{i}
.
Since
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
by
Remark

3.3
, and
\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}
, we get
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
. This implies
i_{1}>\omega_{j}
.
Using
i_{2}\leq\omega_{j}+p-\beta
, we obtain
i_{1}>i_{2}-p+\beta>i_{2}-p
. Moreover,
\omega_{i}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-i_{1}>2\mathbbm{c}-i_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p+\beta=j_{1}+1
.
Therefore,
j_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
implies that
i_{2}\leq j_{1}+2p-\beta+1<\omega_{i}+2p-\beta
. Since
\omega_{j}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\beta}{2}
, by
Proposition

3.1
(iii)
, we get
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}\leq n-2p-1
. Thus,
\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
implies that
\omega_{i}<n-2p-1
. We have
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
. Using
Proposition

3.12
(9)
(9)(ii)
, it follows that
F^{\prime}\prec F
, which is a contradiction.
Hence
\omega_{i}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
.

Now, suppose that
\omega_{i}\geq\omega_{j}
. Then
i_{2}>\omega_{j}>2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. Since
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
by
Remark

3.3
, and
\omega_{i}\geq\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
, we have
i_{2}>\omega_{i}
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
.
We have
i_{2}\leq\omega_{j}+p-\beta\leq\omega_{i}+p-\beta
,
i_{1}\geq j_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
and
i_{1}\geq j_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p+\beta-1\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\beta-1
.
If
\omega_{i}>\omega_{j}
, then
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
implies that
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}\omega_{i}
by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
, and hence
F^{\prime}\ll F
by
Definition

3.4
(ii)
. Moreover, if
\omega_{i}=\omega_{j}
, then
j_{1}<i_{1}
implies that
F^{\prime}\ll F
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
. In either case,
F^{\prime}\ll F
.
It follows that
F^{\prime}\prec F
by
Corollary

3.13
(ii)
, a contradiction. Hence
\omega_{i}<\omega_{j}
.

We conclude that
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}\leq\omega_{i}<\omega_{j}
.
∎

Proposition 3.18
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Let
u\in V(C_{n}^{p})
. If either
u<i_{1}<\omega_{i}
, or
\omega_{i}<i_{2}<u
, then
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
.

Proof.

By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1},i_{2}
. First, suppose
u<i_{1}<\omega_{i}
. If
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
, then
u<\omega_{i}
implies that
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
. Now, let
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}
. Since
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
and
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
, using
Remark

3.2
(ii)
, we get
i_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. Thus
u<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
, which implies that
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
.

Now, suppose
\omega_{i}<i_{2}<u
. Let
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
. Since
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
and
\omega_{i}<i_{2}
, from
Remark

3.2
(ii)
, we get
i_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. Thus
u>2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
, and we get
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
. Now, if
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}
, then
u>\omega_{i}
implies that
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
.
∎

Proposition 3.19
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
with
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
. Let
u\in F
.

(1)

Suppose
u<i_{1}
. Then
({F({u},{i_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{u,i_{2}\}
. Moreover, if either
i_{2}\leq i_{1}+2p
and
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
, or
i_{2}<n-p
and
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
, then
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

(2)

Suppose
u>i_{2}
. Then
({F({u},{i_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},u\}
. Moreover, if either
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p
and
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
, or
i_{1}>p
and
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
, then
{F({u},{i_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

(3)

Let
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
for some
\beta\in[p-1]
and
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
.

(i)

If
u<i_{1}
and
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, then
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\prec F
.

(ii)

If
u>i_{2}
and
{F({u},{i_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, then
{F({u},{i_{2}})}\prec F
.

Proof.

(1)

Since
u<i_{1}<\omega_{i}
, we get
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
by
Proposition

3.18
. Moreover,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
by
Remark

3.3
, and
u<i_{1}<i_{2}
. Thus
({F({u},{i_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{u,i_{2}\}
. First, assume that
i_{2}\leq i_{1}+2p
and
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
. Since
i_{2}>\omega_{i}
, we have
\omega_{i}-i_{2}\ (\text{mod}\ n)=n+\omega_{i}-i_{2}
. Then
i_{2}\leq i_{1}+2p\leq\omega_{i}+2p-1
and
n\geq 6p-3
implies that
\omega_{i}-i_{2}\ (\text{mod}\ n)\geq 4p-2
.
Further,
p\geq 2
implies that
\omega_{i}>i_{2}+2p\ (\text{mod}\ n)
. Therefore, since
u<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
and
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
, we get
u\nsim i_{2}
or
u\nsim\omega_{i}
. This means that
i_{2}
or
\omega_{i}
is an isolated vertex in
C_{n}^{p}[({F({u},{i_{1}})})^{c}]
. Thus
C_{n}^{p}[({F({u},{i_{1}})})^{c}]
is disconnected, and hence
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Now, assume that
i_{2}<n-p
and
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
. We have
u<i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}<n-p
. Since
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
, we get
u\nsim\omega_{i}
and
u\nsim i_{2}
. Hence
u
is an isolated vertex in
C_{n}^{p}[({F({u},{i_{1}})})^{c}]
, and thus
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

(2)

Since
\omega_{i}<i_{2}<u
,
Proposition

3.18
implies
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
. We have
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
by
Remark

3.3
, and
i_{1}<i_{2}<u
.
Thus
({F({u},{i_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},u\}
. First, assume that
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p
and
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
.
Since
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
,
i_{1}\geq i_{2}-2p\geq\omega_{i}-2p+1
and
n\geq 6p-3
, it follows that
i_{1}-\omega_{i}\ (\text{mod}\ n)=n+i_{1}-\omega_{i}\geq 4p-2
.
Further,
p\geq 2
implies that
i_{1}>\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod}\ n)
. Using
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<u
and
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
, we obtain
\omega_{i}\nsim u
or
u\nsim i_{1}
. Hence
{F({u},{i_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Now, assume that
i_{1}>p
and
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
. Since
p<i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}<u
and
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
, we get
u\nsim\omega_{i}
and
u\nsim i_{1}
. Hence
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

(3)

First, let
u<i_{1}
.
Then
({F({u},{i_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{u,i_{2}\}
by
(1)
. Suppose
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\beta\leq\alpha\leq p-1
. Since
u<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
{F({u},{i_{1}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. This implies
u\geq i_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
. It follows that
i_{1}>i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
(as
u<i_{1}
and
\alpha\geq\beta
), a contradiction to our assumption that
i_{1}=i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
.
Therefore,
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime}<\beta
. Hence
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

Now, let
u>i_{2}
.
Then
({F({u},{i_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},u\}
by
(2)
. Suppose
{F({u},{i_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\gamma}
for some
\beta\leq\gamma\leq p-1
. Then
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<u
implies that
{F({u},{i_{2}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}^{8})
. We get
i_{1}\geq u-2p+\gamma-1
. Since
i_{2}<u
and
\gamma\geq\beta
, it follows that
i_{1}>i_{2}-2p+\beta-1
, a contradiction.
Therefore,
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
for some
0\leq\gamma^{\prime}<\beta
. Hence
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

∎

Proposition 3.20
.

Let
F,F^{\prime}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Let
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{j}
for some
i\neq j
such that
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
and
F^{\prime c}=\{\omega_{j}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
with
j_{1}<\omega_{j}<j_{2}
and
j_{1}\leq i_{1}<i_{2}\leq j_{2}
.

(i)

Suppose
\omega_{i}<j_{1}
. Then
(F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j}))^{c}=\{j_{1}\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i},j_{2}\}
. If
j_{2}\leq n-p
and
j_{1}\nsim j_{2}
, then
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

(ii)

Suppose
\omega_{i}>j_{2}
. Then
(F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j}))^{c}=\{j_{2}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},\omega_{i}\}
. If
j_{1}>p
and
j_{1}\nsim j_{2}
, then
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

(iii)

Suppose
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, and either
\omega_{i}<j_{1}
or
\omega_{i}>j_{2}
. If
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
for some
\beta\in[p-1]
and
j_{1}=j_{2}-2p+\beta-1
, then
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\prec F^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By
Remark

3.3
, we have
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1},i_{2}
and
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{1},j_{2}
.

(i)

If
\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}
, then
j_{1}<\omega_{j}
implies
j_{1}<\mathbbm{c}
. If
\omega_{j}\geq\mathbbm{c}
, then by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
,
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{1}
and
j_{1}<\omega_{j}
imply
j_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
, and hence
j_{1}<\mathbbm{c}
. Therefore
\omega_{i}<j_{1}<\mathbbm{c}
. This gives
j_{1}<_{\Omega}\omega_{i}
. We have
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{i}<j_{1}\leq i_{1}
and
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
. By
Remark

3.2
(i)
,
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
.
It follows that
2\mathbbm{c}-j_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}\leq i_{1}<i_{2}\leq j_{2}
. Since
j_{1}<\mathbbm{c}
, we get
j_{1}<_{\Omega}j_{2}
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
. Moreover,
\omega_{i}<j_{1}<j_{2}
implies
\omega_{i}<j_{2}
. Hence
(F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j}))^{c}=\{j_{1}\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i},j_{2}\}
.

Suppose
j_{2}\leq n-p
and
j_{1}\nsim j_{2}
. Observe that since
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1},i_{2}
, using the definition of
\Omega
,
\omega_{i}\neq 0
. This means that
0<\omega_{i}<j_{1}<j_{2}\leq n-p
. Therefore,
j_{1}\nsim j_{2}
implies that
\omega_{i}\nsim j_{2}
. Hence
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

(ii)

If
\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}
, then
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{2}
and
\omega_{j}<j_{2}
imply
j_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
, and hence
j_{2}>\mathbbm{c}
. If
\omega_{j}\geq\mathbbm{c}
, then
\omega_{j}<j_{2}
implies
j_{2}>\mathbbm{c}
. Therefore
\omega_{i}>j_{2}>\mathbbm{c}
. This means that
j_{2}<_{\Omega}\omega_{i}
.
Since
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}
,
i_{2}\leq j_{2}<\omega_{i}
and
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
, by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
,
i_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. This gives
j_{1}\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-j_{2}
. By
Remark

3.2
(ii)
,
j_{2}>\mathbbm{c}
implies
j_{2}<_{\Omega}j_{1}
. Since
j_{1}<j_{2}<\omega_{i}
, we have
j_{1}<\omega_{i}
. Hence
(F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j}))^{c}=\{j_{2}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},\omega_{i}\}
.

Suppose
j_{1}>p
and
j_{1}\nsim j_{2}
. Then
p<j_{1}<j_{2}<\omega_{i}
. Since
j_{1}\nsim j_{2}
, it follows that
\omega_{i}\nsim j_{1}
. Hence
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

(iii)

First, let
\omega_{i}<j_{1}
. By
(i)
,
(F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j}))^{c}=\{j_{1}\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i},j_{2}\}
. Suppose
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\beta\leq\alpha\leq p-1
. Then
\omega_{i}<j_{1}<j_{2}
implies
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. This yields
\omega_{i}\geq j_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
. Since
\omega_{i}<j_{1}
and
\alpha\geq\beta
, we get
j_{1}>j_{2}-2p+\beta-1
, which contradicts our assumption
j_{1}=j_{2}-2p+\beta-1
.
Therefore,
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime}<\beta
. Hence
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

Now, let
\omega_{i}>j_{2}
.
Then
(F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j}))^{c}=\{j_{2}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},\omega_{i}\}
by
(ii)
. Suppose
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in\mathcal{M}_{\gamma}
for some
\beta\leq\gamma\leq p-1
. Since
j_{1}<j_{2}<\omega_{i}
, it follows that
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}^{8})
. We get
j_{1}\geq\omega_{i}-2p+\gamma-1
. Using
j_{2}<\omega_{i}
and
\gamma\geq\beta
, we obtain
j_{1}>j_{2}-2p+\beta-1
, a contradiction.
Therefore,
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in\mathcal{M}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
for some
0\leq\gamma^{\prime}<\beta
. Hence
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\prec F^{\prime}
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

∎

Proposition 3.21
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Let
j_{1},j_{2}\in V(C_{n}^{p})
and
F^{\prime c}=\{\omega_{i},j_{1},j_{2}\}
. If
j_{1}\nsim j_{2}
,
j_{1}<\omega_{i}<j_{2}
and
j_{1}\leq i_{1}<i_{2}\leq j_{2}
, then
F^{\prime}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Proof.

We have
j_{1}\nsim j_{2}
,
j_{1}<\omega_{i}<j_{2}
and
j_{1}\leq i_{1}<i_{2}\leq j_{2}
. Observe that if
\omega_{i}\sim j_{1}
and
\omega_{i}\sim j_{2}
, then
\omega_{i}\sim i_{1}
and
\omega_{i}\sim i_{2}
. This implies
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is connected, a contradiction to
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.
Hence
\omega_{i}\nsim j_{1}
or
\omega_{i}\nsim j_{2}
. Therefore, since
j_{1}\nsim j_{2}
, it follows that
F^{\prime}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.
∎

Proposition 3.22
.

Let
F,F^{\prime}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Let
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{j}
such that
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
and
F^{\prime c}=\{\omega_{j}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
with
j_{1}<\omega_{j}<j_{2}
.
Suppose that either
\omega_{j}<\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
with
\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}
, or
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}\leq\omega_{i}<\omega_{j}
with
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
.

(i)

Then
\omega_{i}\in F^{\prime}\setminus F
,
(F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j}))^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
and
j_{1}<\omega_{i}<j_{2}
.

(ii)

If
j_{1}\leq i_{1}<i_{2}\leq j_{2}
and
j_{1}\nsim j_{2}
, then
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

(iii)

If
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
,
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
for some
\beta\in[p-1]
and
j_{1}=j_{2}-2p+\beta-1
, then
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\prec F^{\prime}
.

Proof.

If
\omega_{j}<\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
with
\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}
, then
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}\omega_{j}
by
Remark

3.2
(iii)
, and if
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}\leq\omega_{i}<\omega_{j}
with
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
, then
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}\omega_{j}
by
Remark

3.2
(iv)
. Hence
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}\omega_{j}
.

(i)

Since
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{1},j_{2}
(
Remark

3.3
) and
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}\omega_{j}
, we get
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}j_{1},j_{2}
. It follows that
\omega_{i}\notin\{\omega_{j},j_{1},j_{2}\}
, and thus
\omega_{i}\in F^{\prime}\setminus F
. Moreover,
(F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j}))^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
.

First, assume that
\omega_{j}<\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
and
