\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}
. Since
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{2}
and
\omega_{j}<j_{2}
, we get
j_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
, which implies
j_{1}<\omega_{j}<\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}\leq j_{2}
.
Now, assume that
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}\leq\omega_{i}<\omega_{j}
and
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
. Since
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{1}
and
j_{1}<\omega_{j}
, we get
j_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
, which implies
j_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}\leq\omega_{i}<\omega_{j}<j_{2}
. In either case,
j_{1}<\omega_{i}<j_{2}
.

(ii)

From
(i)
,
j_{1}<\omega_{i}<j_{2}
. Therefore,
j_{1}\nsim j_{2}
and
j_{1}\leq i_{1}<i_{2}\leq j_{2}
imply
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
by
Proposition

3.21
.

(iii)

We have
(F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j}))^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
and
j_{1}<\omega_{i}<j_{2}
by
(i)
. Suppose
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\beta<\alpha\leq p-1
. Then
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
.
We get
j_{1}\geq j_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
. Since
\alpha>\beta
, it follows that
j_{1}>j_{2}-2p+\beta-1
, a contradiction.
Therefore,
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime}\leq\beta
. Further,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}\omega_{j}
implies that
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\ll F^{\prime}
by
Definition

3.4
(ii)
. Hence
F^{\prime}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\prec F^{\prime}
by
Definition

3.10
.

∎

Proposition 3.23
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Suppose
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
for some
\beta\in[p-1]
such that
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{9})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{10})
. Let
u\in F
.

(i)

If
u<\omega_{i}
, then
F(u,i_{1})\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
F(u,i_{1})\prec F
.

(ii)

If
\omega_{i}<u<i_{1}
,
F(u,i_{1})\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
(F(u,i_{1}))^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{u,i_{2}\}
, then
F(u,i_{1})\prec F
.

Proof.

Since
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{9})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{10})
, it follows that
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
.

(i)

By
Proposition

3.6
(ix)
and
(x)
, we have
\omega_{i}\geq 2p
and
i_{1}\sim i_{2}
. Therefore,
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
implies
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
. Note that
(F(u,i_{1}))^{c}=\{\omega_{i},i_{2},u\}
, where
u<\omega_{i}<i_{2}\leq n-1
and
i_{2}\nsim\omega_{i}
.
If
i_{2}\sim u
and
u\sim\omega_{i}
, then
\omega_{i}\leq i_{2}+2p\ (\text{mod}\ n)\leq 2p-1
, a contradiction. Hence
i_{2}\nsim u
or
u\nsim\omega_{i}
. Thus
F(u,i_{1})\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

We now show that
F(u,i_{1})\prec F
. By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
. Since
u\neq\omega_{i}
, either
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
or
u<_{\Omega}\omega_{i}
.

First, assume that
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
. Then
u<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
and
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
imply
(F(u,i_{1}))^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{u,i_{2}\}
. We show that
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
0\leq\alpha<\beta
, which implies
F\prec F^{\prime}
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
. Suppose
F(u,i_{1})\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\beta\leq\alpha\leq p-1
.
Since
u<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
,
F(u,i_{1})
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. If
F(u,i_{1})
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
, then
i_{2}\sim\omega_{i}
by
Proposition

3.6
(vi)
,
(vii)
and
(viii)
, a contradiction.
Therefore,
F(u,i_{1})
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. This yields
i_{2}\leq u+2p-\alpha+1
. Using
u<\omega_{i}
and
\alpha\geq\beta
, we obtain
i_{2}<\omega_{i}+2p-\beta+1
. If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{9})
, then
i_{2}=\omega_{i}+2p-\beta+1
, a contradiction. Hence
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{10})
.
By
Proposition

3.6
(x)
,
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}
. If
F(u,i_{1})
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
, then
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
; and if
F(u,i_{1})
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
, then
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
by
Proposition

3.6
(iv)
. Both contradict
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}
. Therefore,
F(u,i_{1})\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
0\leq\alpha<\beta
.

Now, assume that
u<_{\Omega}\omega_{i}
. Since
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
, we get
u<_{\Omega}i_{2}
, and thus
\omega_{i}<i_{2}
implies
(F(u,i_{1}))^{c}=\{u\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i},i_{2}\}
. Suppose
F(u,i_{1})\in\mathcal{M}_{\gamma}
for some
\gamma\in[p-1]
. Then
u<\omega_{i}<i_{2}
implies that
F(u,i_{1})
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}^{9})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\gamma}^{10})
. By
Proposition

3.6
(ix)
and
(x)
,
i_{2}\sim\omega_{i}
, a contradiction. Hence
F(u,i_{1})\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and thus
F(u,i_{1})\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(ii)

By
Definition

3.4
(i)
,
u<i_{1}
implies
F(u,i_{1})\ll F
. Suppose
F(u,i_{1})\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\beta<\alpha\leq p-1
. We have
\omega_{i}<u<i_{2}
. If
\omega_{i}\leq n-2p-2
, then
F(u,i_{1})
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
and
F_{i}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{9})
. This implies
i_{2}=\omega_{i}+2p-\alpha+1<\omega_{i}+2p-\beta+1=i_{2}
, a contradiction.
If
\omega_{i}>n-2p-2
, then
F(u,i_{1})
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
and
F_{i}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{10})
. This implies
i_{2}=n-\alpha-1<n-\beta-1=i_{2}
, again a contradiction. Therefore,
F(u,i_{1})\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime}\leq\beta
.
Since
F(u,i_{1})\ll F_{i}
,
\alpha^{\prime}\leq\beta
implies
F(u,i_{1})\prec F_{i}
by
Definition

3.10
.

∎

We now show that
\prec
provides a shelling order for the facets of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
. By the definition of shellability, it suffices to prove that for any
F_{r},F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
with
F_{r}\prec F_{s}
, there is an
F_{t}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that

(
\ast
)

F_{t}\prec F_{s}\text{ and }F_{t}\cap F_{s}=F_{s}\setminus\{u\}\text{ for some }u\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}.\hfill

Let
F_{r},F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F_{r}\prec F_{s}
. Let
F_{r}\in\mathcal{A}_{r_{0}}
and
F_{s}\in\mathcal{A}_{s_{0}}
, where
F_{r}^{c}=\{\omega_{r_{0}}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},r_{2}\}
and
F_{s}^{c}=\{\omega_{s_{0}}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
.
To simplify the notation, we write
\omega_{r}
and
\omega_{s}
for
\omega_{r_{0}}
and
\omega_{s_{0}}
, respectively. We have
r_{1}<r_{2}
and
s_{1}<s_{2}
. Moreover,
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}r_{1},r_{2}
and
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{1},s_{2}
by
Remark

3.3
. We aim to find an
F_{t}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F_{t}
satisfies (

∗

‣
3.1
) for the pair
F_{r}\prec F_{s}
.

Remark 3.24
.

For
u\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
and
v\in F_{s}^{c}
, if
{F_{s}({u},{v})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
{F_{s}({u},{v})}\prec F_{s}
, then
F_{t}={F_{s}({u},{v})}
satisfies (

∗

‣
3.1
) for the pair
F_{r}\prec F_{s}
.

We separately deal with the cases
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. The former case is further divided into two subcases:
r_{0}=s_{0}
(
Lemma

3.25
) and
r_{0}\neq s_{0}
(
Lemma

3.28
). The latter case is addressed in
Lemma

3.31
.

Lemma 3.25
.

Suppose
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. If
F_{r}\in\mathcal{A}_{s_{0}}
(i.e.,
r_{0}=s_{0}
), then there exists a facet
F_{t}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
that satisfies (

∗

‣
3.1
) for the pair
F_{r}\prec F_{s}
.

Proof.

Since
F_{r}\in\mathcal{A}_{s_{0}}
, we have
\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}
. This means that
F_{r}^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},r_{2}\}
and
F_{s}^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
. Moreover,
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}r_{1},r_{2},s_{1},s_{2}
. Observe that if
\{r_{1},r_{2}\}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\emptyset
, then (

∗

‣
3.1
) is satisfied by taking
F_{t}=F_{r}
. Henceforth, we assume that
\{r_{1},r_{2}\}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\emptyset
. So
r_{1},r_{2}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
.

We have
F_{r}\prec F_{s}
and
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. By
Definition

3.10
,
F_{r}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F_{r}\ll F_{s}
. Therefore,
\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}
implies that either
r_{1}<s_{1}
, or
r_{1}=s_{1}
and
r_{2}<s_{2}
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
.
Since
r_{1},r_{2},s_{1}
and
s_{2}
are distinct, we get
r_{1}<s_{1}<s_{2}
. Recall that
r_{1}<r_{2}
. We now consider the following cases: (I)
s_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{s}
, (II)
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
, (III)
\omega_{s}<s_{1}<s_{2}
.

(I)

s_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{s}
.

We show that
F_{t}={F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies (

∗

‣
3.1
). We have
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}=(F_{s}\setminus\{r_{1}\})\sqcup\{s_{1}\}
and
r_{1}<s_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{s}
. Since
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}r_{1},s_{2}
, we get
({F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})})^{c}=(F_{s}^{c}\setminus\{s_{1}\})\sqcup\{r_{1}\}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},s_{2}\}
.
By
Proposition

3.11
(iii)
for
F_{s}
, we get
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
. Suppose
r_{1}\sim\omega_{s}
. Then
r_{1}<s_{1}<\omega_{s}
and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
imply
r_{1}\leq\omega_{s}+p\ (\text{mod}\ n)
.
Further, since
F_{r}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
,
C_{n}^{p}[F_{r}^{c}]
is disconnected. Therefore
r_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
, which implies
r_{2}<\omega_{s}
. Using
Proposition

3.11
(iii)
for
F_{r}
, we get
r_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
, a contradiction. Hence
r_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
.

If
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
, then
C_{n}^{p}[{({F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}})^{c}]
is disconnected and hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Now, let
s_{2}\sim\omega_{s}
.
Since
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
,
C_{n}^{p}[F_{r}^{c}]
is disconnected. This implies
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
, and therefore
r_{1}\nsim s_{2}
. Thus
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Since
r_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{s}
,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
.
First, suppose that
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
. Then
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
r_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
.
Since
s_{1}>r_{1}
, we get
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha
. By
Proposition

3.12
(1)
(1)(ii)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
. Then
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
r_{1}=\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha-1
.
Since
s_{1}>r_{1}
, we get
s_{1}\geq\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha
. By
Proposition

3.12
(2)
(2)(ii)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, again a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
.

Therefore
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Since
r_{1}<s_{1}
, we have
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\ll F_{s}
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
. Hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
. We have
r_{1}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. Thus
F_{t}={F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies (

∗

‣
3.1
) by
Remark

3.24
.

(II)

s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
.

We have
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}=(F_{s}\setminus\{r_{1}\})\sqcup\{s_{2}\}
,
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}r_{1},s_{1}
and
r_{1}<s_{1}
. It follows that
({F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},s_{1}\}
. There are two possibilities: either
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
or
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

(a)

{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

We first assume that
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Since
r_{1}<s_{1}
, we get
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\ll F_{s}
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
, and therefore
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
. We have
r_{1}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. Hence, by taking
F_{t}={F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}
, (

∗

‣
3.1
) is satisfied by
Remark

3.24
.

Now, assume that
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. We show that
F_{t}={F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies (

∗

‣
3.1
).

Since
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, it follows that
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Hence,
r_{1}<s_{1}<\omega_{s}
implies
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
. By
Proposition

3.6
(i)
and
(ii)
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
.
If
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
, then
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
r_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
; and if
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
, then
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
r_{1}=\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha-1
. In either case,
r_{1}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}+\alpha-1
, which implies
r_{1}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{3p}{2}
. By
Proposition

3.1
(v)
,
r_{1}\geq p
. Since
r_{1}<s_{1}<\omega_{s}
and
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, we get
r_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
by
Proposition

3.11
(iii)
. Therefore,
p\leq r_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}\leq n-1
implies
r_{1}\nsim s_{2}
.
Using
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}r_{1},s_{2}
and
r_{1}<s_{2}
, we obtain
({F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},s_{2}\}
. Observe that
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Claim 3.26
.

{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

Proof of
3.26
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
for some
\beta\in[p-1]
.
We have
({F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})})^{c}

=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},s_{2}\}
,
r_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
and
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
. By
Proposition

3.8
, it follows that
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{8})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{6})
. Then
r_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\beta-1
and
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-1
.
Since
s_{1}>r_{1}
,
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\beta
. Moreover,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
implies
s_{2}<\omega_{s}+p
. By
Proposition

3.6
(vi)
,
\omega_{s}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p-2}{2}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
. Thus

by
Proposition

3.12
(6)
(6)(ii)
, a contradiction.
Hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{6})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
. Then
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
,
r_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\beta-1
and
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p\leq s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p-\beta
. Since
s_{1}>r_{1}
,
s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\beta
. Note that
s_{2}<\omega_{s}+p
. By
Proposition

3.12
(7)
(7)(ii)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{8})
. Then
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
r_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\beta-1
. Since
s_{1}>r_{1}
, we get
s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\beta
.
By
Proposition

3.12
(8)
(8)(ii)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{8})
.

We conclude that
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. This completes the proof of
3.26
.
∎

Since
r_{1}<s_{1}
, we have
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\ll F_{s}
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
. Hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
. Thus,
F_{t}={F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies (

∗

‣
3.1
) by
Remark

3.24
(as
r_{1}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
).

(b)

{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

In this case,
C_{n}^{p}[({F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})})^{c}]
is connected. We have

Lemma 3.28
.

Suppose

. If

∗

. By
Definition

3.10
,
F_{r}\prec F_{s}
and
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
imply
F_{r}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
F_{r}\ll F_{s}
. Therefore
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s}
by
Definition

3.4
.
Recall that
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{1},s_{2}
. It follows that
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}s_{1},s_{2}
. Hence
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
.

For
v\in F_{s}^{c}
, we have
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{v})})^{c}=(F_{s}^{c}\setminus\{v\})\sqcup\{\omega_{r}\}
. Since
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s},s_{1},s_{2}
, we get
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{v})}\in\mathcal{A}_{r_{0}}
, and thus
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{v})}\ll F_{s}
by
Definition

3.4
(ii)
.
Therefore, if
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{v})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}\subseteq M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, then
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{v})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
, and hence
F_{t}={F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{v})}
satisfies (

∗

‣
3.1
) by
Remark

3.24
.

In this proof, we show that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{v})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
for some
v\in F_{s}^{c}
.
Clearly,
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s}
implies that
\omega_{s}\neq\mathbbm{c}
. Therefore, we have two cases: (A)
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
and (B)
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
.

Case A:

\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
.

Since
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s}
implies, we get
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
by
Remark

3.2
(iii)
.

(I)

s_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{s}
.

By
Proposition

3.11
(iii)
for
F_{s}
, we have
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
. Moreover,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{A}_{r_{0}}
and
s_{1}<s_{2}
imply
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
.

We first show that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Since
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
,
C_{n}^{p}[F_{s}^{c}]
is disconnected. Therefore
\omega_{s}\geq p+2
, and hence
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-p-2\leq n-p-1
(as
2\mathbbm{c}\leq n+1
). It follows that
s_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq n-p-1
.
Since
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
, we get
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
. If
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
, then
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Now, let
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
. Then
C_{n}^{p}[F_{s}^{c}]
is disconnected implies
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
, and thus
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{r}
. Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

We now show that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Since
s_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{r}
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
. Note that
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{s}
imply
s_{1}<\omega_{s}-p
.
If
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
, then
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1
. Using
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, we obtain
s_{1}>\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1\geq\omega_{s}-p
, a contradiction. Further, if
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
, then
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}=\omega_{r}-2p+\alpha-1
. It follows that
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p>\omega_{s}-p
, again a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

(II)

s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
.

Since
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{2}
,
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
and
s_{2}>\omega_{s}
,
Remark

3.2
(i)
implies
s_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
. Thus
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq s_{2}
. Observe that
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
. We show that if
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, then
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Otherwise,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

(a)

{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Since
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
. Then
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{2}=s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
. Since
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(3)
(3)(i)
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
. Then
\omega_{r}-p+\alpha\leq s_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p-1
and
s_{2}=s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
. If
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
, then
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(3)
(3)(i)
, a contradiction.
Hence
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
.
Since
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}
, we have
\omega_{s}-p<\omega_{s}-p+\alpha<s_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p-1
. Therefore
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(4)
(4)(i)
, again a contradiction. It follows that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
s_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-\alpha
and
s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\alpha=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p
. Since
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, it follows that
s_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
and
s_{1}>\omega_{s}-p
. Moreover, we have
s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha
.
By
Corollary

3.13
(i)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
. Then
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1
and
s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-1
. If
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
, then
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(3)
(3)(ii)
, a contradiction. Hence
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
.
Since
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, we have
s_{1}>\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1\geq\omega_{s}-p
and
s_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-1
.
Now, if
s_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p-1
, then
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(4)
(4)(ii)
; and if
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p
, then
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(5)
(5)(ii)
(for
\beta=1
). Each case contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
. Then
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
and
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p\leq s_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p-\alpha
.
Observe that if
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
, then
Proposition

3.12
(3)
(3)(i)
contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Hence
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
.
Since
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, we get
\omega_{s}+p<s_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
. It follows that
\omega_{s}-p<\omega_{s}-p+\alpha\leq s_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p-1
.
By
Proposition

3.12
(4)
(4)(i)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, again a contradiction.
Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. Then
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
.
Since
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, we have
\omega_{s}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
. Therefore,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(3)
(3)(i)
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
.

We conclude that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

(b)

{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

We first prove that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. We have
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
. Since
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
,
C_{n}^{p}[({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}]
is connected. If
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{r}
, then
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
and
s_{1}\sim\omega_{r}
, which implies
s_{1}\sim\omega_{s}
. This contradicts
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.
Hence
s_{2}\sim\omega_{r}
. Since
C_{n}^{p}[F_{s}^{c}]
is disconnected,
s_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p
.

Suppose
s_{1}\sim\omega_{r}
. It follows that
s_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-p
or
s_{1}\leq\omega_{r}+p\ (\text{mod}\ n)
. First, let
s_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-p
. Then
s_{1}>\omega_{s}-p
. Moreover,
s_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p
implies
s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p=s_{1}+2p-1+1
. Since
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, we have
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-1
. By
Corollary

3.13
(i)
(for
\beta=1
),
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, a contradiction.
Hence
s_{1}\leq\omega_{r}+p\ (\text{mod}\ n)
. Using
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
, we get
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
and
\omega_{r}\geq n-p
. Thus
n-p\leq\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq n+1-\omega_{s}
(as
2\mathbbm{c}\leq n+1
). This gives
\omega_{s}<p+1
, which implies

s_{1}\sim\omega_{s}
, contradicting
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Therefore
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
.

Since
C_{n}^{p}[({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}]
is connected and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
, it follows that
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
. Therefore
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
implies
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
.
Using
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{A}_{r_{0}}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{s}
, we obtain
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},\omega_{s}\}
. Observe that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

We now show that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Then
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}
implies
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
.
If
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
, then
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1
. Since
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, we get
s_{1}>\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1\geq\omega_{s}-p
, which contradicts
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
. This gives
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}=\omega_{r}-2p+\alpha-1
.
It follows that
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1>\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
, again contradicting
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
.
Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

(III)

\omega_{s}<s_{1}<s_{2}
.

Recall that
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
. By
Remark

3.2
(i)
,
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{1}
and
s_{1}>\omega_{s}
imply
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
.
Thus,
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq s_{1}<s_{2}
. Moreover,
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
by
Proposition

3.11
(iv)
for
F_{s}
. Since
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{A}_{r_{0}}
and
\omega_{s}<s_{2}
, we have
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{\omega_{s},s_{2}\}
.

We first show that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. If
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{r}
, then
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
implies
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. So, let
s_{2}\sim\omega_{r}
. Since
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
, we get
s_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p
. It follows that
s_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p\leq s_{1}+p
, and hence
s_{1}>s_{2}-p
.
Suppose
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p+1
. Then
\omega_{s}<s_{1}<s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p+1
, which implies
s_{1}\sim\omega_{s}
and
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
, contradicting
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Hence
s_{2}\geq\omega_{s}+p+2
. Using
s_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p
, we obtain
\omega_{s}+p+2<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p
. This yields
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}-1=\mathbbm{c}-\frac{1+1}{2}
.
By
Proposition

3.1
(iii)
,
\omega_{s}<n-2p-1
. Therefore, if
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+2p=\omega_{s}+2p-1+1
, then
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(9)
(for
\beta=1
), a contradiction. Hence
s_{2}>\omega_{s}+2p
.
Since
s_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p
, we have
\omega_{r}>\omega_{s}+p
, which implies
\omega_{s}\nsim\omega_{r}
(as
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
). Therefore,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Claim 3.29
.

{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

Proof of
3.29
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Then
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
implies that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
. Then
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{2}=\omega_{s}+2p-\alpha+1
. Note that
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}>2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}>\omega_{r}+p>\omega_{s}+p
. Since
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
,
s_{1}>\omega_{s}+p
. By
Proposition

3.1
(ii)
,
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}\leq n-2p-1
, which implies
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<n-2p-1
. Therefore,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(9)
(9)(i)
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
. Then
\omega_{s}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p-1
and
s_{2}=\omega_{s}+2p-\alpha+1
. By
Proposition

3.6
(iv)
,
\omega_{r}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
.
Therefore, since
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}
,
Proposition

3.1
(iii)
implies
\omega_{s}<n-2p-1
. We have
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\geq\omega_{r}+p+1>\omega_{s}+p+1
.
Thus
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(9)
(9)(i)
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
\omega_{r}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
,
s_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-\alpha
and
\omega_{s}\geq s_{2}-2p+\alpha
. Since
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}
,
Proposition

3.1
(iii)
implies
\omega_{s}<n-2p-1
. Using
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, we get
s_{1}>2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}=s_{2}-p+\alpha>s_{2}-p
.
Therefore
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(9)
(9)(ii)
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
. Then
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
\omega_{s}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1
and
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-1
.
It follows that
s_{2}>s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}=\omega_{r}+p-\alpha+1\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha}{2}+p+1\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p+1
, a contradiction.
Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
. Then
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
,
\omega_{s}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
and
s_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p-\alpha
. Observe that
\omega_{r}\geq\omega_{s}+p+1
. Since
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}>\omega_{r}
, we have
s_{1}>\omega_{s}+p+1
. Moreover,
\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}-p-1<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
implies
\omega_{s}<n-p-1
by
Proposition

3.1
(ii)
.
Therefore,
Proposition

3.12
(9)
(9)(i)
contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. Then
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
\omega_{s}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
. Since
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, it follows that
\omega_{s}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}\leq n-2p-1
by
Proposition

3.1
(ii)
.
Moreover,
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
implies
s_{1}>\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}+p\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p>\omega_{s}+p
. Thus
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(9)
(9)(i)
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
.

Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
∎

Case B:

\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
.

Since
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s}
, we get
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
by
Remark

3.2
(iv)
.

(I)

s_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{s}
.

We have
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{A}_{r_{0}}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{s}
. Hence
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},\omega_{s}\}
.
Since
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{2}
and
s_{2}<\omega_{s}
,
Remark

3.2
(ii)
implies
s_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
.
It follows that
s_{1}<s_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
.
By
Proposition

3.11
(iii)
for
F_{s}
,
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
.

If
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
, then
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
implies
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Now, let
s_{1}\sim\omega_{r}
. Since
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
, we get
s_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-p
.
This gives
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+1-1
. Observe that if
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
Proposition

3.12
(1)
(for
\beta=1
) contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
Hence
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
. Now, if
s_{1}\geq\omega_{s}-2p=\omega_{s}-2p+1-1
, then
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(2)
(for
\beta=1
), again a contradiction. Therefore
s_{1}<\omega_{s}-2p
.
Since
s_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-p
, we have
\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}-p
, which implies
\omega_{s}\nsim\omega_{r}
(as
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
). Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Claim 3.30
.

{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

Proof of
3.30
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Then
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
implies
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
. Then
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
and
\omega_{s}=s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
.
Since
\omega_{r}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
,
\omega_{s}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}>\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
. Moreover,
s_{1}=\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha-1
.
Therefore
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(2)
(2)(i)
, a contradiction.
Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
. Then
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
,
s_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-p+\alpha
and
\omega_{s}=s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
.
This implies
\omega_{s}\geq\omega_{r}+p+1>\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}=\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha-1
.
By
Proposition

3.12
(2)
(2)(i)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, a contradiction.
Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
\omega_{r}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
,
\omega_{s}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-\alpha
and
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p
. It follows that
s_{1}<s_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}=\omega_{r}-p+\alpha\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha+1}{2}-p-1\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p-1
, a contradiction.
Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
. Then
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1
and
\omega_{s}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha
. Since
\omega_{s}>\omega_{r}
, we have
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha
.
If
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
Proposition

3.12
(1)
(1)(ii)
contradicts
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Hence
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
. Note that
s_{1}\geq\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha
.
By
Proposition

3.12
(2)
(2)(ii)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, again a contradiction. Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
. Then
s_{1}=\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha-1
and
\omega_{s}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p
.
It follows that
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1
. Since
\omega_{s}>\omega_{r}
, we get
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha
.
If
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(1)
(1)(ii)
, a contradiction.
Hence
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
. Then
s_{1}=\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha-1
implies
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(2)
(2)(i)
, again a contradiction. Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. Then
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}=\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha-1
. Since
\omega_{s}>\omega_{r}
, we get
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.
Therefore,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(2)
(2)(i)
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
.

Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
∎

(II)

s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
.

Recall that
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
. Since
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{1}
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{s}
, we get
s_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
. Thus
s_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
.
Observe that
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
. We show that if
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, then
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Otherwise,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

(a)

{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Then
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
implies
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
. Then
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{2}=s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
.
Since
\omega_{r}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
,
\omega_{s}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}>\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
. Moreover,
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
. By
Proposition

3.12
(8)
(8)(i)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
. Then
\omega_{r}-p+\alpha\leq s_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p-1
and
s_{2}=s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
. If
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
Proposition

3.12
(8)
(8)(i)
contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Hence
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.
Since
\omega_{r}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, we get
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha\leq s_{1}\leq\omega_{s}-p-1
. It follows that
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p+1\leq s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p-\alpha<\omega_{s}+p
.
By
Proposition

3.12
(7)
(7)(i)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, again a contradiction. Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
s_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-\alpha
and
s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\alpha=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p
.
If
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(8)
(8)(ii)
, a contradiction. Hence
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.
Since
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
, we get
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p-\alpha<\omega_{s}+p
and
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+1
.
Now, if
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-1
, then
Proposition

3.12
(6)
(6)(ii)
(for
\beta=1
) contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
; and if
s_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p
, then
Proposition

3.12
(7)
(7)(ii)
contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
. Then
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1
and
s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-1
.
Since
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
, we get
s_{1}>2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
and
s_{2}<\omega_{s}+p
. By
Corollary

3.13
(ii)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
. Then
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
and
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p\leq s_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p-\alpha
.
Since
\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
,
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p<s_{2}<\omega_{s}+p-\alpha<\omega_{s}+p
. If
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
Proposition

3.12
(7)
(7)(i)
contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
; and
if
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
Proposition

3.12
(8)
(8)(i)
contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. Then
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
.
Since
\omega_{s}>\omega_{r}
,
Proposition

3.12
(8)
(8)(i)
contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
.

Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

(b)

{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

In this case, we show that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. We have
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
. Since
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
,
C_{n}^{p}[({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}]
is connected. If
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
, then
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
and
s_{2}\sim\omega_{r}
, which implies
s_{2}\sim\omega_{s}
. This contradicts
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.
Hence
s_{1}\sim\omega_{r}
. Since
C_{n}^{p}[F_{s}^{c}]
is disconnected,
s_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-p
.

Suppose
s_{2}\sim\omega_{r}
. It follows that
s_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p
or
s_{2}\geq\omega_{r}-p\ (\text{mod}\ n)
.
First, let
s_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p
. Then
s_{2}<\omega_{s}+p
. Moreover,
s_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-p
implies
s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p=s_{2}-2p+1-1
. Since
\omega_{r}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, we have
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+1-1
. By
Corollary

3.13
(ii)
,
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, a contradiction. Hence
s_{2}\geq\omega_{r}-p\ (\text{mod}\ n)
.
Since
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
, we get
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
and
\omega_{r}\leq p-1
. This gives
n-\omega_{s}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}\leq p-1
(as
2\mathbbm{c}\geq n
), and thus
\omega_{s}\geq n-p+1
.
Therefore
s_{2}\sim\omega_{s}
, contradicting
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Hence
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{r}
.

Since
C_{n}^{p}[({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}]
is connected and
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{r}
, we get
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
. Then
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
implies
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
.
We have
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{A}_{r_{0}}
and
\omega_{s}<s_{2}
. Hence
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{\omega_{s},s_{2}\}
. Observe that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Since
\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
.
By
Proposition

3.6
(ix)
and
(x)
,
s_{2}\sim\omega_{s}
, a contradiction. Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

(III)

\omega_{s}<s_{1}<s_{2}
.

We have
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
. Moreover,
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
. Since
C_{n}^{p}[F_{s}^{c}]
is disconnected,
\omega_{s}\leq n-p-3
. This implies
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-n+p+3\geq p+3
(as
2\mathbbm{c}\geq n
), and hence
p+3\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}<s_{1}<s_{2}
.
By
Proposition

3.11
(iv)
for
F_{s}
,
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
, which implies
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{r}
. If
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
, then
{{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Now, let
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
. Since
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
,
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
. It follows that
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
. Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Since
\omega_{r}<s_{1}<s_{2}
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
. Then
\omega_{r}\leq n-2p-2
,
s_{1}\geq\omega_{r}+p+1
and
s_{2}=\omega_{r}+2p-\alpha+1
. This gives
s_{1}\geq s_{2}-p+\alpha>s_{2}-p
and
s_{2}\leq n-\alpha-1
. Since
\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
, we have
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+2p-\alpha
. If
\omega_{s}\leq n-2p-2
, then
Proposition

3.12
(9)
(9)(ii)
contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
; and if
\omega_{s}>n-2p-2
, then
Proposition

3.12
(10)
contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
. Then
\omega_{r}>n-2p-2
,
s_{1}\geq\omega_{r}+p+1
and
s_{2}=n-\alpha-1
.
It follows that
s_{1}>n-p-1=s_{2}-p+\alpha>s_{2}-p
. Since
\omega_{s}>\omega_{r}>n-2p-2
,
Proposition

3.12
(10)
(10)(i)
contradicts
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
.

Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

∎

Lemma 3.31
.

Suppose
F_{s}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, i.e.,
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Then there exists a facet
F_{t}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
that satisfies (

∗

‣
3.1
) for the pair
F_{r}\prec F_{s}
.

Proof.

We have
F_{r}^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},r_{2}\}
and
F_{s}^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
with
F_{r}\prec F_{s}
. Moreover,
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}r_{1},r_{2}
and
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{1},s_{2}
.
Observe that if
\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}
and
\{r_{1},r_{2}\}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}\neq\emptyset
, then
F_{t}=F_{r}
satisfies (

∗

‣
3.1
). Therefore, we assume that
\{r_{1},r_{2}\}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\emptyset
whenever
\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}
.

Recall that
{F_{s}({u},{v})}=(F_{s}\setminus\{u\})\sqcup\{v\}
, where
u\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
and
v\in F_{s}^{c}
. If
{F_{s}({u},{v})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F_{s}({u},{v})}\prec F_{s}
, then
F_{t}={F_{s}({u},{v})}
satisfies (

∗

‣
3.1
) by
Remark

3.24
.
Thus, it suffices to show that there exist
u\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
and
v\in F_{s}^{c}
such that
{F_{s}({u},{v})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F_{s}({u},{v})}\prec F_{s}
.
Since
F_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
,
F_{s}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
.

(1)

F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
. We first prove the following claim.

Claim 3.32
.

s_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{s}
,
\mathbbm{c}<\omega_{s}<n-p
and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
.

Proof of
3.32
.

Since
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
,
s_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{s}
. Hence
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
by
Proposition

3.11
(iii)
. If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
, then
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
by
Proposition

3.6
(i)
; and if
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
, then
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
by definition. Thus
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
. Moreover,
Proposition

3.6
(i)
and
(ii)
imply
\omega_{s}<n-p
.
∎

We consider three cases: (I)
r_{1}<s_{1}
, (II)
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\omega_{s}
, (III)
s_{1}\leq r_{1}
and
\omega_{s}<r_{2}
.

(I)

Let
r_{1}<s_{1}
. Since
s_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{s}
by
3.32
,
r_{1}<s_{1}<\omega_{s}
. By
Proposition

3.18
,
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}r_{1}
. Observe that
r_{1}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
and
({F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},s_{1}\}
.
By
3.32
,
\omega_{s}<n-p
and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
, which implies
r_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
. Therefore
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

We now show that
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\prec F_{s}
. Suppose
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}\leq p-1
. We have
r_{1}<s_{1}<\omega_{s}
.
If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
, then
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
. Since
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{1})
, which implies
r_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha^{\prime}-1\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1=s_{1}
, a contradiction.
If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
, then
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}=\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha-1
. Since
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2})
, which implies
r_{1}=\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha^{\prime}-1\geq\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha-1=s_{1}
, again a contradiction.
Therefore,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}<\alpha
.
Hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(II)

Let
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\omega_{s}
. By
3.32
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
. Hence
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}<\omega_{s}
. We consider the following subcases based on the value of
\omega_{r}
:

(a)

\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
, then
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
. Moreover, if
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
, then
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}=\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha-1
, which yields
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
. Since
r_{1}\geq s_{1}
and
\alpha\geq 1
, we obtain
r_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1>\omega_{r}-p
in both cases.
Therefore,
C_{n}^{p}[F_{r}^{c}]
is disconnected and
r_{1}<r_{2}
imply
r_{2}>\omega_{r}
. Using
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
and
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
, we get
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}
. By
Remark

3.2
(i)
,
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}r_{2}
implies
r_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
, and hence
r_{2}>\omega_{s}
. This contradicts
r_{2}\leq\omega_{s}
. Thus
\omega_{r}\not<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
.

(b)

2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
.

Using
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{1}
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}
and
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
Remark

3.2
(ii)
implies
s_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
. Hence
s_{1}<\omega_{r}
. Further, since
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s}
by
Remark

3.2
(iv)
. Then
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{1},s_{2}
implies
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}s_{1},s_{2}
. Thus
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
and
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},\omega_{s}\}
.

Claim 3.33
.

{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Proof of
3.33
.

By
3.32
,
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
. We have either
r_{1}<r_{2}<\omega_{r}
or
r_{1}<\omega_{r}<r_{2}
or
\omega_{r}<r_{1}<r_{2}
.

First, let
r_{1}<r_{2}<\omega_{r}
. Then
r_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
by
Proposition

3.11
(iii)
. Since
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}<\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
, we get
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Now, let
r_{1}<\omega_{r}<r_{2}
. Since
F_{r}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, we have
r_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
or
r_{2}\nsim\omega_{r}
. Using
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<\omega_{r}<r_{2}\leq\omega_{s}
and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
, it follows that if
r_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
, then
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
; and if
r_{2}\nsim\omega_{r}
, then
\omega_{s}\nsim\omega_{r}
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Finally, let
\omega_{r}<r_{1}<r_{2}
. Then
r_{2}\nsim\omega_{r}
by
Proposition

3.11
(iv)
. Since
s_{1}<\omega_{r}
, we get
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\omega_{s}
. Therefore
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
implies
\omega_{s}\nsim\omega_{r}
, and hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.
This completes the proof of
3.33
.
∎

We now show that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\prec F_{s}
. Since
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s}
, we get
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{2}})}\ll F_{s}
by
Definition

3.4
(ii)
. First, assume that
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
. Then
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1

3.10

(c)

\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}
.

(1)(ii)

(2)

Claim 3.34
.

s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
,
s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1\leq s_{1}+2p
,
s_{1}>p
,

,
s_{1}\sim\omega_{s}
,

and

.

Proof of
3.34
.

Since
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
.
If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
, then
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
; and if
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
, then
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
by
Proposition

3.6
(iv)
.
Moreover, if
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
, then
s_{2}=s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
; and if
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
, then
s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\alpha
. Hence, in all these three cases, we get
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
and
s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1\leq s_{1}+2p
.
Using
Proposition

3.6
(iii)
,
(iv)
and
(v)
, it follows that
s_{1}>p
,
s_{2}\leq n-p
and
s_{1}\sim\omega_{s}
. Therefore,
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
implies
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
and
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
.
∎

We consider three cases: (I)
r_{1}<s_{1}
, (II)
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
, (III)
s_{1}\leq r_{1}
and
s_{2}<r_{2}
.

(I)

Let
r_{1}<s_{1}
. By
3.34
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p
and
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
. Hence
r_{1}<s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
, which implies
r_{1}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. Moreover,
({F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},s_{2}\}
and
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
by
Proposition

3.19
(1)
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
, then
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
. Hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Proposition

3.19
(3)
(3)(i)
. Now, let
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
\omega_{s}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
,
s_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
, and by
Proposition

3.6
(v)
,
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p-1}{2}
.
Suppose
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha<\alpha^{\prime}\leq p-1
. Since
r_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
and
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p-1}{2}\leq\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
. If
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
, then
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
by
Proposition

3.6
(vii)
, a contradiction. Now, if
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
, then by
Proposition

3.6
(iv)
,
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha^{\prime}
; and if
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})
, then
s_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha^{\prime}
. In both cases,
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha^{\prime}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
, a contradiction.
Therefore,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}\leq\alpha
. By
Definition

3.4
(i)
,
r_{1}<s_{1}
implies
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\ll F_{s}
, and hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
.

(II)

Let
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
. By
3.34
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
and
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
. Since
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{2}
,
Remark

3.2
(i)
implies
s_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
. Moreover,
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}>\omega_{s}
. Thus
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq s_{2}
. We deal with the following subcases based on the value of
\omega_{r}
:

(a)

\omega_{r}<s_{1}
.

We have
\omega_{r}<s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
. Clearly,
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. By
3.34
,
s_{2}\leq n-p
and
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
. Therefore, by
Proposition

3.20
(i)
,
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{s_{1}\}\sqcup\{\omega_{r},s_{2}\}
and
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
, then
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
, which implies
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Proposition

3.20
(iii)
.

Now, let
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
\omega_{s}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
and
s_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
. Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}\leq p-1
. Since
\omega_{r}<s_{1}<s_{2}
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{8})
.
It follows that
s_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+2p-\alpha^{\prime}+1
. Using
r_{2}\leq s_{2}
and
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}
, we get
r_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+2p-\alpha+1
. By
Proposition

3.1
(iii)
,
\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
implies
\omega_{r}\leq n-2p-2
.
Since
\omega_{r}<s_{1}\leq r_{1}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
, we have
\omega_{r}<r_{1}
and
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}
. By
Remark

3.2
(i)
,
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}r_{1}
implies
r_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
. This gives
r_{2}\leq s_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-\alpha\leq r_{1}+p-\alpha
, and hence
r_{1}>r_{2}-p
. Note that
\omega_{r}<r_{1}<r_{2}
.
Moreover,
\omega_{r}<s_{1}<\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
implies
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}\omega_{r}
. By
Definition

3.4
(ii)
,
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
. Therefore,
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Proposition

3.12
(9)
, a contradiction.
This means that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}<\alpha
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

(b)

s_{1}\leq\omega_{r}\leq\omega_{s}
.

From
3.34
,
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{s}<s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
,
s_{1}\sim\omega_{s}
and
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
. If
\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
, then
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
implies
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}\omega_{r}
, and hence
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
by
Definition

3.4
(ii)
. Moreover, if
\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}
, then
\{r_{1},r_{2}\}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\emptyset
by our assumption, which implies
s_{1}<r_{1}
, and thus
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
. In either case,
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
.

Claim 3.35
.

r_{1}\geq\omega_{r}+p+1
.

Proof of
3.35
.

Recall that
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
. Suppose
r_{1}<\omega_{r}
. Then
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<\omega_{r}\leq\omega_{s}<s_{2}
. Since
s_{1}\sim\omega_{s}
and
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
, we get
s_{1}\geq\omega_{s}-p
, which implies
r_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-p
. By
Proposition

3.11
(i)
,
r_{2}>\omega_{r}
. Thus
\omega_{r}-p\leq r_{1}<\omega_{r}<r_{2}
. We have
r_{2}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1\leq r_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
and
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
. Observe that if
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
, then
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Proposition

3.12
(3)
, a contradiction. So
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
.
Since
\omega_{s}\geq\omega_{r}
,
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. By
Remark

3.7
(i)
,
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
. Using
r_{2}\leq s_{2}
and
\omega_{r}\leq\omega_{s}
, we obtain
r_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-\alpha
.
By
Corollary

3.13
(i)
,
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
, again a contradiction. Hence
r_{1}>\omega_{r}
. By
Remark

3.2
(i)
,
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}r_{1}
and
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}
imply
r_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
, then
\omega_{r}\leq\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
, which implies
r_{1}>\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}>\omega_{r}+p
.
Now, let
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
r_{1}<r_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-\alpha\leq r_{1}+p-\alpha
, which implies
r_{1}\sim r_{2}
. Since
C_{n}^{p}[F_{r}^{c}]
is disconnected and
r_{1}>\omega_{r}
, we get
r_{1}\geq\omega_{r}+p+1
. Thus
r_{1}\geq\omega_{r}+p+1
.
∎

We have
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
,
r_{2}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1\leq\omega_{r}+2p-\alpha+1
and by
3.35
,
\omega_{r}<\omega_{r}+p+1\leq r_{1}<r_{2}
.
If
\omega_{r}\leq n-2p-2
, then
Proposition

3.12
(9)
contradicts
F_{r}\prec F_{s}
. Hence
\omega_{r}>n-2p-2
. It follows that
s_{2}\geq r_{2}>r_{1}\geq\omega_{r}+p+1\geq n-p
. Further,
n\geq 6p-3
implies
s_{2}>5p-3
.
Since
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}\leq n-2p-1
by
Proposition

3.1
(ii)
, we get
\omega_{s}\geq\omega_{r}\geq n-2p-1\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
. Therefore,
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. By
Remark

3.7
(i)
,
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
. This implies
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-\alpha\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-n+3p-\alpha+1
. Using
2\mathbbm{c}\leq n+1
and
\alpha\geq 1
, it follows that
s_{2}\leq 3p+1
. Thus
5p-3<s_{2}\leq 3p+1
, which contradicts
p\geq 2
. Hence, this case is not possible.

(c)

\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
.

Recall that
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
. By
3.34
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
and
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
.
Hence
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
,
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
by
Proposition

3.22
(i)
. Since
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
by
Proposition

3.22
(ii)
. We show that

{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
, then
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
, which implies
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Proposition

3.22
(iii)
.

Now, let
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p
,
s_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
and by
Proposition

3.6
(v)
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
.
Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha<\alpha^{\prime}\leq p-1
.
Since
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}<\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
, it follows that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})
. Then
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-\alpha^{\prime}
by
Remark

3.7
(i)
. Since
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}
and
\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha
, we get
s_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
, a contradiction.
Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
and
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{6})
. Then
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{2}
and
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-1
.
Since
\omega_{r}>\mathbbm{c}
,
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}<\omega_{r}
. This implies
r_{2}\leq s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-1<\omega_{r}+p
.
We have
r_{1}\geq s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p>\omega_{r}-p
. Thus
\omega_{r}-p<r_{1}<r_{2}<\omega_{r}+p
. This contradicts
C_{n}^{p}[F_{r}^{c}]
is disconnected. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{6})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
. Then
s_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p-\alpha^{\prime}
. Since
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
and
\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha
, it follows that
s_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
.

Therefore,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}\leq\alpha
. Since
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
and
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
,
Remark

3.2
(iii)
implies
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s}
. By
Definition

3.4
(ii)
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\ll F_{s}
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
.

(d)

2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}\leq s_{2}
.

We have
s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
and
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
by
3.34
. Since
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}\omega_{r}
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
. Hence
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
by
Definition

3.4
(ii)
. Moreover,
s_{1}\leq r_{1}
implies
s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1\leq r_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
, then
s_{1}\geq\omega_{s}-p+\alpha
by
Proposition

3.6
(iii)
. If
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
, then
s_{1}\geq\omega_{s}-p+\alpha
by definition. If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
, then
\omega_{s}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
and
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p
, which implies
s_{1}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha+1}{2}-p\geq\omega_{s}-p+\alpha+1
.
Thus
s_{1}\geq\omega_{s}-p+\alpha
. Since
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}
, we get
r_{1}\geq s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha
.

Suppose
r_{2}<\omega_{r}
. Then
r_{1}<r_{2}<\omega_{r}
. If
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
r_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha
implies
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Proposition

3.12
(1)
(1)(ii)
, a contradiction. So,
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.
Using
\omega_{r}\leq s_{2}\leq r_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
, we get
r_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-2p+\alpha-1
. Since
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
,
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Proposition

3.12
(2)
, again a contradiction. Hence
r_{2}>\omega_{r}
.

Since
r_{2}\leq s_{2}
and
s_{2}\leq r_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
, we get
r_{1}\geq r_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
.

We now show that
r_{2}<\omega_{r}+p
. First, suppose
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
. Then
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{2}=s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1
. It follows that
\omega_{r}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}>\omega_{s}+p
. Since
s_{1}<\omega_{s}
, we get
r_{2}\leq s_{2}=s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1\leq\omega_{s}+2p-\alpha<\omega_{r}+p-\alpha
. If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
, then
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
by
Remark

3.7
(i)
, which implies
r_{2}\leq s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha\leq\omega_{r}+p-\alpha
. Thus
r_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p-\alpha<\omega_{r}+p
.

Since
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
, it follows that
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Corollary

3.13
(ii)
, a contradiction. Therefore, this case is not possible.

(e)

\omega_{r}>s_{2}
.

By
3.34
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
and
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
. Clearly,
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. Suppose
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
\omega_{s}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}
,
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p
and
s_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
. Since
\alpha\leq p-1
, we have
\omega_{r}>s_{2}>2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
. By
Remark

3.2
(ii)
,
r_{2}\leq s_{2}<\omega_{r}
and
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}r_{2}
imply
r_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
. It follows that
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p\leq s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}-p+\alpha
.
This gives
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha}{2}
, a contradiction. Therefore,
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
, and hence
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
.
Moreover,
s_{1}>p
and
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
by
3.34
. Thus,
Proposition

3.20
(ii)
and
(iii)
yield
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
, respectively.

(III)

Let
s_{1}\leq r_{1}
and
s_{2}<r_{2}
. From
3.34
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
,
s_{1}>p
and
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
. Note that
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<r_{2}
and
r_{2}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. By
Proposition

3.19
(2)
,
({F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},r_{2}\}
and
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{4})
, then
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
, and thus
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Proposition

3.19
(3)
(3)(ii)
. Now, let
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{5})
. Then
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p
and
s_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
. By
Proposition

3.6
(v)
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
.
Suppose
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}\leq p-1
. Since
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<r_{2}
and
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}<\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
,
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
. If
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
, then
s_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p-1
, a contradiction. If
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
, then
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
by
Proposition

3.6
(vii)
, again a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})
. This gives
s_{2}<r_{2}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha^{\prime}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
, a contradiction.
Therefore,
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}<\alpha
. By
Definition

3.10
(ii)
,
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}\prec F_{s}
.

(3)

F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
.

Claim 3.36
.

s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1\geq s_{2}-2p
,
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
,
s_{1}>p
,
s_{2}\leq n-p
,
s_{2}\sim\omega_{s}
,
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
.

Proof of
3.36
.

Since
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
, then
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha
and by
Proposition

3.6
(vi)
,
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p-\alpha-1
.
If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
, then
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
,
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
, and by
Proposition

3.6
(vii)
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
.
If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
, then
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
, and by
Proposition

3.6
(viii)
,
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
.
Hence, in all these three cases, we get
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1\geq s_{2}-2p
and
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
.

Using
Proposition

3.6
(vi)
,
(vii)
and
(viii)
, it follows that
s_{1}>p
,
s_{2}\leq n-p
and
s_{2}\sim\omega_{s}
. Therefore,
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
implies
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
.
∎

We consider three cases: (I)
r_{1}<s_{1}
, (II)
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
, (III)
s_{1}\leq r_{1}
and
s_{2}<r_{2}
.

(I)

Let
r_{1}<s_{1}
. By
3.36
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
s_{2}\leq n-p
and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
. Note that
r_{1}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
.

(a)

Let
s_{2}<n-p
. Since
r_{1}<s_{1}
and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
,
Proposition

3.19
(1)
implies
({F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})})^{c}

=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},s_{2}\}
and
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
, then
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
, and hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Proposition

3.19
(3)
(3)(i)
. Now, let
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
.
Then
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
,
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-1
and by
Proposition

3.6
(vi)
,
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.
Suppose
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}\leq p-1
. Since
r_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
and
\mathbbm{c}<\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
.
If
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
, then
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
by
Proposition

3.6
(iv)
, a contradiction. If
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
, then
s_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p
, again a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{6})
. This gives
s_{1}>r_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha^{\prime}-1\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
, a contradiction.
Therefore,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}<\alpha
. By
Definition

3.10
(ii)
,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
.

(b)

Let
s_{2}=n-p
. From
Proposition

3.6
(vi)
and
Proposition

3.6
(vii)
,
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. By
Proposition

3.6
(viii)
,
n-p=s_{2}\leq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{3p}{2}-1
. Since
\mathbbm{c}\leq\frac{n+1}{2}
,
n\leq 5p-1
. Therefore,
n\geq 6p-3
implies
p\leq 2
. Hence
p=2
(as
p\geq 2
), and thus
\alpha=1
.

By
Proposition

3.18
,
r_{1}<s_{1}<\omega_{s}
implies
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}r_{1}
. Hence
({F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},s_{1}\}
.
Since
0\leq r_{1}<s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}=n-p
and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
, we get
r_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
. Therefore
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. This means that
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha^{\prime}\in[0,p-1]=\{0,1\}
.
If
\alpha^{\prime}=0
, then
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
. Now, assume
\alpha^{\prime}=1
. Then
\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha
. Since
r_{1}<s_{1}
,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\ll F_{s}
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
.
Hence
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{2}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
(i)
.

(II)

Let
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
. From
3.36
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
and
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
. Since
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{1}
,
Remark

3.2
(i)
implies
s_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
. Moreover,
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}<\omega_{s}
. Thus
s_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
. We consider the following subcases based on the value of
\omega_{r}
:

(a)

\omega_{r}<s_{1}
.

Suppose
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
. Then
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
and
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-1
. We have
\omega_{r}<s_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
. By
Remark

3.2
(i)
,
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}r_{1}
and
\omega_{r}<s_{1}\leq r_{1}
imply
r_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
. Moreover,
\omega_{s}+p-\alpha+1\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-s_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
.
Therefore
\omega_{s}+p-\alpha+1<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-1
, which gives
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha-2}{2}
, a contradiction. Hence
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. This means that
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
.
From
3.36
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
s_{2}\leq n-p
and
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
. Observe that
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. By
Proposition

3.20
(i)
and
(iii)
, we obtain
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
, respectively.

(b)

s_{1}\leq\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
.

We have
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
and
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
by
3.36
. From
Remark

3.2
(ii)
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
implies
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}\omega_{r}
. By
Definition

3.4
(ii)
,
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
.

Claim 3.37
.

r_{1}\geq\omega_{r}+p+1
.

Proof of
3.37
.

We first show that
r_{1}>\omega_{r}-p
. If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
, then
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
by
Remark

3.7
(ii)
, which implies
r_{1}\geq s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1\geq\omega_{r}-p+\alpha
. Now, suppose
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. Then
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
. It follows that
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}\leq\omega_{s}-p
. Since
\omega_{s}<s_{2}
, we get
r_{1}\geq s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1\geq\omega_{s}-2p+\alpha>\omega_{r}-p+\alpha
. Thus,
r_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-p+\alpha>\omega_{r}-p
.

Suppose
r_{1}<\omega_{r}
. Then
\omega_{r}-p<r_{1}<\omega_{r}
. Since
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
, it follows that
r_{1}\geq s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1\geq r_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
and
r_{2}\leq s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p-\alpha<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}+p-\alpha
.
We have
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
. By
Corollary

3.13
(i)
,
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
, a contradiction. Hence
r_{1}>\omega_{r}
.

Since
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}r_{1}
and
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
,
Remark

3.2
(i)
implies
r_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
.
Thus
\omega_{s}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p-\alpha
. It follows that
r_{1}\sim r_{2}
. Since
C_{n}^{p}[F_{r}^{c}]
is disconnected and
r_{1}>\omega_{r}
, we get
r_{1}\geq\omega_{r}+p+1
.
∎

We have
\omega_{r}<\omega_{r}+p+1\leq r_{1}<r_{2}
,
r_{2}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{1}+2p-\alpha+1\leq\omega_{r}+2p-\alpha+1
and
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
. If
\omega_{r}\leq n-2p-2
, then
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Proposition

3.12
(9)
, a contradiction. So, assume
\omega_{r}>n-2p-2
.
By
Proposition

3.1
(ii)
,
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}\leq n-2p-1
. Hence
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}>\omega_{r}\geq n-2p-1\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
, which implies
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
. This means that
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
.
If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
, then
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
; and if
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
, then
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
by
Proposition

3.6
(vii)
. Thus
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
.
By
Proposition

3.1
(iii)
,
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq n-2p-1
, a contradiction. Hence
\omega_{r}\not>n-2p-2
.
Therefore, this case is not possible.

(c)

2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
.

We have
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
. Moreover,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
and
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
by
3.36
.
Therefore,
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
,
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
by
Proposition

3.22
(i)
. Since
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
by
Proposition

3.22
(ii)
. We now show that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
, then
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
, which implies
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Proposition

3.22
(iii)
.

Now, let
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
. Then
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
and
s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-1
. From
Proposition

3.6
(vi)
,
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.
Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha<\alpha^{\prime}\leq p-1
. Since
\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
, it follows that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
. Then
s_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-p+\alpha^{\prime}
. Since
\omega_{r}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
and
\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha
, it follows that
s_{1}>2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})
. Then
\omega_{r}\leq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}+1}{2}<\mathbbm{c}
and
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p
.
Since
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}
,
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}>\omega_{r}
. This implies
r_{1}\geq s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p>\omega_{r}-p
.
We have
r_{2}\leq s_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p-1<\omega_{r}+p
. Thus
\omega_{r}-p<r_{1}<r_{2}<\omega_{r}+p
. This contradicts
C_{n}^{p}[F_{r}^{c}]
is disconnected. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})
.
