Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
. Then
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha^{\prime}-1
by
Remark

3.7
(ii)
. Since
\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
and
\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha
, we get
s_{1}>2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
, a contradiction. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
does not satisfy
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{6})
and
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7})
.

Therefore,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}\leq\alpha
. Since
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s}
by
Remark

3.2
(iv)
. By
Definition

3.4
(ii)
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\ll F_{s}
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
.

(d)

\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}\leq s_{2}
.

From
3.36
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{s}>s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1,s_{2}\sim\omega_{s}
and
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
. If
\omega_{r}>\omega_{s}
, then
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
implies
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}\omega_{r}
, and hence
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
by
Definition

3.4
(ii)
. Moreover, if
\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}
, then
\{r_{1},r_{2}\}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\emptyset
, which implies
s_{1}<r_{1}
, and thus
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
. In either case,
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
.

Suppose
r_{2}>\omega_{r}
. Then
s_{1}<\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
. Since
s_{2}\sim\omega_{s}
and
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
, we get
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+p
, and hence
r_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p
. By
Proposition

3.11
(ii)
,
r_{1}<\omega_{r}
. Thus
r_{1}<\omega_{r}<r_{2}\leq\omega_{r}+p
. We have
r_{1}\geq s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1\geq r_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
and
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
. Observe that if
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Proposition

3.12
(8)
, a contradiction. So
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.
Since
\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}
,
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
. By
Remark

3.7
(ii)
,
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
. Using
s_{1}\leq r_{1}
and
\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}
, we obtain
r_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1
. Therefore,
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Corollary

3.13
(ii)
, again a contradiction. Hence
r_{2}<\omega_{r}
. This gives
r_{1}<r_{2}<\omega_{r}
.

Note that
r_{1}\geq s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1\geq\omega_{r}-2p+\alpha-1
. If
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Proposition

3.12
(2)
, a contradiction. So
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.
Then
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
. We get
r_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1
. This implies
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Proposition

3.12
(1)
, again a contradiction. Hence, this case is not possible.

(e)

\omega_{r}>s_{2}
.

From
3.36
,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
s_{1}>p
and
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
. Observe that
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. By
Proposition

3.20
(ii)
,
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{s_{2}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},\omega_{r}\}
and
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
, then
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
, which implies
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Proposition

3.20
(iii)
.

Now, let
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
.
Then
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
and
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
. Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}\leq p-1
. Since
s_{1}<s_{2}<\omega_{r}
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{8})
.
This implies
s_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-2p+\alpha^{\prime}-1
. Using
s_{1}\leq r_{1}
and
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}
, we get
r_{1}\geq\omega_{r}-2p+\alpha-1
.
Moreover,
\omega_{r}>\omega_{s}
implies
r_{1}\geq s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1>2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\alpha-1
.
Since
\omega_{r}>s_{2}>\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}\omega_{r}
. By
Definition

3.4
(ii)
,
F_{s}\ll F_{r}
. We have
r_{1}<r_{2}<\omega_{r}
(as
r_{2}\leq s_{2}<\omega_{r}
).
Observe that if
\omega_{r}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Proposition

3.12
(1)
(1)(ii)
, and if
\omega_{r}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
by
Proposition

3.12
(2)
. Both cases contradict
F_{r}\prec F_{s}
.
Therefore,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}<\alpha
. By
Definition

3.10
(ii)
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
.

(III)

Let
s_{1}\leq r_{1}
and
s_{2}<r_{2}
. We have
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
s_{1}\geq s_{2}-2p
,
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
and
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
by
3.36
. Note that
r_{2}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
.
By
Proposition

3.19
(2)
,
({F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},r_{2}\}
and
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{7})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
, then
s_{1}=s_{2}-2p+\alpha-1
, which implies that
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Proposition

3.19
(3)
(3)(ii)
.

Now, let
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{6})
. Then
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}
,
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
and by
Proposition

3.6
(vi)
,
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.
Since
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
,
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha^{\prime}\in[0,p-1]
.
If
0\leq\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha
, then
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{2}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

So, assume that
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}\leq p-1
.

Claim 3.38
.

{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
.

Proof of
3.38
.

We have
\mathbbm{c}+\frac{\alpha}{2}\leq\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.
Recall that if
\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}
, then
\{r_{1},r_{2}\}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\emptyset
, which implies
s_{1}<r_{1}
. Moreover,
s_{1}<\omega_{s}<r_{2}
,
s_{1}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}
and
r_{2}\in F_{s}
. By
Proposition

3.17
for
F=F_{r}
and
F^{\prime}=F_{s}
, we get
r_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p
and
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
. Since
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
,
Proposition

3.22
(i)
yields
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
,
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
and
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
.

Note that
\omega_{r}<s_{2}<r_{2}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}+p\leq\omega_{r}+p
. By
Proposition

3.11
(ii)
,
r_{1}\leq\omega_{r}-p-1
. This gives
s_{1}\leq r_{1}\leq\omega_{r}-p-1<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
. Since
s_{1}\nsim s_{2}
, we get
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{r}
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta}
for some
\alpha<\beta\leq p-1
. Then
s_{1}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
and
\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
implies that
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
. If
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{3})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{4})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{5})
, then using
Proposition

3.6
(iii)
,
(iv)
and
(v)
we get
s_{1}\sim\omega_{r}
, a contradiction.
Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{6})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\beta}^{7})
. By
Remark

3.7
(ii)
,
s_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}-p+\beta-1
. Using
\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
and
\alpha<\beta
, we get
s_{1}>2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
, a contradiction as
s_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}-p+\alpha-1
.
Therefore,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\beta^{\prime}}
for some
0\leq\beta^{\prime}\leq\alpha
. Since
\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
and and
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}\leq\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
,
Remark

3.2
(iv)
implies
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s}
.
By
Definition

3.4
(ii)
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\ll F_{s}
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{\omega_{s}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
.
∎

This completes the case.

(4)

F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
.

Claim 3.39
.

\omega_{s}<s_{1}<s_{2}
,
\omega_{s}\geq 2p
,
s_{1}\geq\omega_{s}+p+1
,
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
,
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
and
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
.

Proof of
3.39
.

Since
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
,
\omega_{s}<s_{1}<s_{2}
and
s_{1}\geq\omega_{s}+p+1
. By
Proposition

3.6
(ix)
and
(x)
,
\omega_{s}\geq 2p
and
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
. Therefore,
F_{s}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
implies
s_{1}\nsim\omega_{s}
and
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
.
∎

We consider three cases: (I)
r_{1}<\omega_{s}
, (II)
\omega_{s}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
, (III)
\omega_{s}\leq r_{1}
and
s_{2}<r_{2}
.

(I)

Let
r_{1}<\omega_{s}
. Then
r_{1}<\omega_{s}<s_{1}<s_{2}
implies
r_{1}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. By
Proposition

3.23
(i)
,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
.

(II)

Let
\omega_{s}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
. We consider the following subcases based on the value of
\omega_{r}
:

(a)

\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}
.

Since
\omega_{r}<\omega_{s}<s_{1}<s_{2}
, we have
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. By
Proposition

3.23
(i)
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
.

(b)

\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}
.

In this case,
\{r_{1},r_{2}\}\cap\{s_{1},s_{2}\}=\emptyset
by our assumption. Hence
r_{1}\notin\{s_{1},s_{2}\}
, and
r_{2}\leq s_{2}
implies
r_{2}<s_{2}
. Moreover, since
\omega_{s}=\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}r_{1}
and
\omega_{s}\leq r_{1}
, we have
\omega_{s}<r_{1}
. Therefore,
\omega_{s}<r_{1}<r_{2}<s_{2}
and
r_{1}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. Observe that
({F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{r_{1},s_{2}\}
. By
3.39
,
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
.
If
\omega_{s}\sim r_{1}
and
r_{1}\sim s_{2}
, then
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
implies
r_{1}\sim r_{2}
. This contradicts
F_{r}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
(as
\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}
). Hence
\omega_{s}\nsim r_{1}
or
r_{1}\nsim s_{2}
. Using
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
, we get
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Suppose
r_{1}>s_{1}
. Since
s_{1}\geq\omega_{s}+p+1
by
3.39
,
r_{1}>\omega_{s}+p+1=\omega_{r}+p+1
. We have
r_{1}<r_{2}<\omega_{r}
.
If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
, then
\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}\leq n-2p-2
and
r_{2}<s_{2}=\omega_{s}+2p-\alpha+1=\omega_{r}+2p-\alpha+1
. By
Proposition

3.12
(9)
(9)(ii)
,
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
, a contradiction.
If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
, then
\omega_{r}=\omega_{s}>n-2p-2
and
r_{2}<s_{2}=n-\alpha-1
. By
Proposition

3.12
(10)
(10)(ii)
,
F_{s}\prec F_{r}
, again a contradiction. Hence
r_{1}<s_{1}
. By
Proposition

3.23
(ii)
,
{F_{s}({r_{1}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
.

(c)

\omega_{r}>\omega_{s}
.

We deal with three subcases:

(i)

Let
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
and
\omega_{r}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
. Then
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s}
by
Remark

3.2
(iii)
. Since
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{1},s_{2}
, we get
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}s_{1},s_{2}
. Hence
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. Observe that
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{r}\}\sqcup\{\omega_{s},s_{2}\}
.
By
Remark

3.2
(i)
,
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}s_{2}
and
s_{2}>\omega_{s}
imply
s_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
. Thus
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
. We have
\omega_{s}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
. Since
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
by
3.39
,
Proposition

3.21
yields
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.
If
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
, then
Proposition

3.6
(x)
contradicts
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
. Hence
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
, which implies
s_{2}=\omega_{s}+2p-\alpha+1
. Suppose
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha<\alpha^{\prime}\leq p-1
. Since
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{8})
.
It follows that
s_{2}\leq\omega_{s}+2p-\alpha^{\prime}+1<\omega_{s}+2p-\alpha+1
, a contradiction. Therefore,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}\leq\alpha
. By
Proposition

3.6
(x)
,
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}\omega_{s}
implies
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\ll F_{s}
. Therefore
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
.

(ii)

Let
\omega_{s}<\mathbbm{c}
and
\omega_{r}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}
. Then
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{s}>\mathbbm{c}
. Clearly,
\omega_{r}>\mathbbm{c}
and
\omega_{s}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
.
We have
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}\leq\omega_{s}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
. Since
\omega_{r}<_{\Omega}r_{1}
,
\omega_{r}>\mathbbm{c}
and
r_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
,
Remark

3.2
(ii)
yields
r_{1}>\omega_{r}
.
By
3.39
,
s_{1}\sim s_{2}
and
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
. Therefore,
\omega_{s}<s_{1}<s_{2}
implies
s_{2}\leq s_{1}+p
. If
\omega_{r}\geq s_{1}
, then
\omega_{s}<s_{1}\leq\omega_{r}<r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
, which contradicts
C_{n}^{p}[F_{r}^{c}]
is disconnected. Hence
\omega_{r}<s_{1}
. This means that
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<s_{1}<s_{2}
, and thus
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
.
By
Remark

3.2
(iv)
,
\omega_{r}>\mathbbm{c}
and
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}\leq\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}
imply
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}\omega_{r}
. Therefore,
({F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{\omega_{r},s_{2}\}
.
We have
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
,
\omega_{s}<\omega_{r}<s_{2}
and
\omega_{s}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq s_{2}
. By
Proposition

3.21
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Hence
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Proposition

3.23
(ii)
.

(iii)

Let
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}
. Then
\omega_{r}>\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}
implies
\omega_{r}>\mathbbm{c}
. Moreover,
\omega_{s}\geq\mathbbm{c}>2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
, which gives
\omega_{s}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
. Using the inequalities
\omega_{r}>\mathbbm{c}
and
\omega_{s}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{r}
, the remainder of the argument proceeds exactly as in case (ii). This gives
\omega_{r}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
,
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F_{s}({\omega_{r}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
.

(III)

Let
\omega_{s}\leq r_{1}
and
s_{2}<r_{2}
. Then
\omega_{s}<s_{1}<s_{2}<r_{2}
implies
r_{2}\in F_{s}\setminus F_{r}
. Since
\omega_{s}<s_{2}
,
\omega_{s}<_{\Omega}r_{2}
by
Proposition

3.18
. Thus
({F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{s}\}\sqcup\{s_{2},r_{2}\}
. Using
p<2p\leq\omega_{s}<s_{2}<r_{2}
and
s_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
, we get
r_{2}\nsim\omega_{s}
. Hence
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Suppose
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}\leq p-1
. We have
\omega_{s}<s_{2}<r_{2}
. If
\omega_{s}\leq n-2p-2
, then
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{9})
and
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
. This implies
r_{2}=\omega_{s}+2p-\alpha^{\prime}+1\leq\omega_{s}+2p-\alpha+1=s_{2}
, a contradiction. If
\omega_{s}>n-2p-2
, then
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{1}})}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{10})
and
F_{s}
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
. This implies
r_{2}=n-\alpha^{\prime}-1\leq n-\alpha-1=s_{2}
, again a contradiction. Therefore
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha^{\prime\prime}<\alpha
. Hence
{F_{s}({r_{2}},{s_{1}})}\prec F_{s}
by
Definition

3.10
(ii)
.

∎

Using
Lemmas

3.25
,
3.28
and
3.31
, we conclude that the order
\prec
provides a shelling order for the facets of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
.

3.2.
Spanning Facets

Having proved that
\prec
provides a shelling order for the facets of
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
, we now characterize and count the spanning facets. We continue to use the notation and results established prior to
Section

3.1
.

We first define several subsets of
V(C_{n}^{p})
.
Let
\mathcal{U}_{1}=\{p+1,p+2,\dots,2p-1\}
,
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\{2p,2p+1,\dots,\mathbbm{c}-p\}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}=\{\mathbbm{c}-p+1,\mathbbm{c}-p+2,\dots,n-p-1\}
. Clearly,
\mathcal{U}_{1}
,
\mathcal{U}_{2}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}
are pairwise disjoint.
For each
u\in\mathcal{U}_{1}\sqcup\mathcal{U}_{2}\sqcup\mathcal{U}_{3}
, define
\mathcal{V}^{u}
as follows:

(1)

If
u\in\mathcal{U}_{1}
, then
\mathcal{V}^{u}=\{u-2p\ (\text{mod}\ n),u-2p+1\ (\text{mod}\ n),\dots,n-1\}\sqcup\{0,1,\dots,2\mathbbm{c}-u-1\}
.

(2)

If
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
, then
\mathcal{V}^{u}=\{u-2p,u-2p+1,\dots,u-2\}\sqcup\{u,u+1,\dots,2\mathbbm{c}-u-1\}\sqcup\{n-1\}
.

(3)

If
u\in\mathcal{U}_{3}
, then
\mathcal{V}^{u}=\{\mu,\mu+1,\dots,u,u+1,\dots,u+p\}\sqcup\{u+t\ (\text{mod $n$})|\ p+1\leq t\leq 2p-1\}\sqcup\{\nu\}
,
where
\mu=\min\{2\mathbbm{c}-u,u-2p\}
and
\nu=\begin{cases}n-1&\text{if }u<n-2p-1,\\
u+2p\ (\text{mod}\ n)&\text{if }u\geq n-2p-1.\end{cases}

Recall that the set of facets
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{p}^{n}))\subset\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{n-2}\mathcal{A}_{i}
, where the sets
\mathcal{A}_{i}
are defined in
Equation

1
. Let
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}
and
\Sigma_{3}
be subsets of
M(\Delta_{3}(C_{p}^{n}))
defined as follows: for
m\in[3]
, a facet
F\in\Sigma_{m}
if and only if
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
for some
i\in[n-2]
such that
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},n-1\}
,
\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{m},
and
i_{1}\in V(C_{p}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}
.

Since
\mathcal{U}_{1}
,
\mathcal{U}_{2}
and
\mathcal{U}_{3}
are pairwise disjoint, it follows that
\Sigma_{1}
,
\Sigma_{2}
and
\Sigma_{3}
are pairwise disjoint. Let

\Sigma:=\Sigma_{1}\sqcup\Sigma_{2}\sqcup\Sigma_{3}.

Our aim is to show that a facet
F
is a spanning facet if and only if
F\in\Sigma
. For this, we begin with some important results.

Proposition 3.40
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. If
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in[p-1]
, then
F^{c}=\{u,v,w\}
for some
u,v,w\in V(C_{n}^{p})
with
u<v<w\leq u+2p
.

Proof.

Let
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Since
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
,
F
satisfies one of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
.
First, suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
. Then
i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}
. If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{1})
, then
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
and
i_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p+\alpha-1
. Using
\alpha\geq 1
, we get
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-p>\omega_{i}-2p
.
If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{2})
, then
i_{1}=\omega_{i}-2p+\alpha-1\geq\omega_{i}-2p
. It follows that
i_{1}<i_{2}<\omega_{i}\leq i_{1}+2p
.
Now, suppose
F
satisfies any of the conditions from
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{3})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{8})
. Then
i_{1}<\omega_{i}<i_{2}\leq i_{1}+2p-\alpha+1\leq i_{1}+2p
.
Finally, suppose
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
or
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
. Then
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}
. If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{9})
, then
\omega_{i}=i_{2}-2p+\alpha-1\geq i_{2}-2p
.
If
F
satisfies
(\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}^{10})
, then
\omega_{i}>n-2p-2
and
i_{2}=n-\alpha-1
, which implies
i_{2}\leq n-2<\omega_{i}+2p
. We get
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{2}\leq\omega_{i}+2p
.

Therefore
F^{c}=\{u,v,w\}
for some
u,v,w\in V(C_{n}^{p})
with
u<v<w\leq u+2p
.
∎

This proposition implies the following corollary.

Corollary 3.41
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. If there exist
u,v\in F^{c}
with
u<v
and
v-u>2p
, then
F\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

For
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, recall that
{F({u},{v})}=(F\setminus\{u\})\sqcup\{v\}
for some
u\in F
and
v\in F^{c}
.

Proposition 3.42
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},n-1\}
. For each
u\in F
, it follows that
{F({u},{n-1})}\ll F
.

Proof.

We have
({F({u},{n-1})})^{c}=(F^{c}\setminus\{n-1\})\sqcup\{u\}=\{\omega_{i},i_{1},u\}
and
u\neq\omega_{i}
.
Hence, either
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
or
u<_{\Omega}\omega_{i}
.

First, let
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
. By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
. This implies
{F({u},{n-1})}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
. If
u<i_{1}
, then
({F({u},{n-1})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{u,i_{1}\}
, and
{F({u},{n-1})}\ll F
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
.
If
u>i_{1}
, then
({F({u},{n-1})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},u\}
, and thus
u<n-1
implies that
{F({u},{n-1})}\ll F
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
.

Now, let
u<_{\Omega}\omega_{i}
. Then
u=\omega_{j}
for some
j<i
by the definition of
<_{\Omega}
, and
{F({u},{n-1})}\in\mathcal{A}_{j}
. Thus,
Definition

3.4
(ii)
implies that
{F({u},{n-1})}\ll F
.
∎

By the definition of spanning facets, a facet
F
is spanning if and only if for each
u\in F
, there exists
v\in F^{c}
such that

(
\#
)

{F({u},{v})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))\text{ and }{F({u},{v})}\prec F.\hfill

The following results are used to prove that if a facet
F\in\Sigma
, then
F
is spanning.

Proposition 3.43
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},n-1\}
. Suppose the following conditions hold:
(a)

\omega_{i}>p
,
(b)

i_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})|-2p\leq t\leq 2p-1\}
, and
(c)
either
i_{1}\neq\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})
, or if
i_{1}=\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})
, then
\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})=\omega_{i}+2p
.
Then
F
is a spanning facet.

Proof.

Note that
i_{1}\notin\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})|-2p\leq t\leq 2p-1\}
and
i_{1}<n-1
. To prove that
F
is a spanning facet, for each
u\in F
, we need to find
v\in F^{c}
such that
{F({u},{v})}
satisfies (

#

‣
3.2
).
Let
u\in F
. We have
n-1\in F^{c}
and
({F({u},{n-1})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i},i_{1},u\}
. By
Proposition

3.42
,
{F({u},{n-1})}\ll F
.

First, suppose that
i_{1}\neq\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})
. Then
i_{1}\notin\{\omega_{i}-2p\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{i}-2p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})\}
.
This implies
i_{1}\nsim\omega_{i}
, and
u\nsim\omega_{i}
or
u\nsim i_{1}
. Hence
{F({u},{n-1})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Since
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
by
Remark

3.3
,
i_{1}\neq\omega_{i}
. Observe that if
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
, then
i_{1}<\omega_{i}-2p
; and if
i_{1}>\omega_{i}
, then
i_{1}>\omega_{i}+2p
.
In either case, we have
F,{F({u},{n-1})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Corollary

3.41
. By
Definition

3.10
(i)
,
{F({u},{n-1})}\ll F
implies
{F({u},{n-1})}\prec F
. Hence (

#

‣
3.2
) is satisfied by
{F({u},{n-1})}
. Therefore
F
is a spanning facet.

Now suppose that
i_{1}=\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})
. Then
\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})=\omega_{i}+2p
. Since
\omega_{i}>p
, it follows that
p<\omega_{i}<\omega_{i}+2p=i_{1}<n-1
. Hence
\omega_{i}\nsim i_{1}
and
\omega_{i}<i_{1}
. Clearly,
u\neq\omega_{i},i_{1}
. We consider three cases: (i)
u<\omega_{i}<i_{1}
, (ii)
\omega_{i}<u<i_{1}
, (iii)
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<u
.

(i)

Let
u<\omega_{i}<i_{1}
. Since
i_{1}\notin\{\omega_{i}-2p\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{i}-2p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{i}-1\ (\text{mod $n$})\}
, we have
u\nsim\omega_{i}
or
u\nsim i_{1}
. Hence
\omega_{i}\nsim i_{1}
implies
{F({u},{n-1})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.
From
Corollary

3.41
,
i_{1}=\omega_{i}+2p>u+2p
implies
{F({u},{n-1})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. Since
{F({u},{n-1})}\ll F
,
{F({u},{n-1})}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
. Therefore
{F({u},{n-1})}
satisfies (

#

‣
3.2
).

(ii)

Let
\omega_{i}<u<i_{1}
. We have
i_{1}\in F^{c}
and
({F({u},{i_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i},u,n-1\}
. Using
p<\omega_{i}<u<i_{1}=\omega_{i}+2p<n-1
, we get
\omega_{i}\nsim n-1
, and
u\nsim n-1
or
u\nsim\omega_{i}
.
Therefore
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Since
\omega_{i}+2p<n-1
,
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Corollary

3.41
.
If
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
, then
u<i_{1}
implies
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\ll F
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
; and if
u<_{\Omega}\omega_{i}
, then
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\ll F
by
Definition

3.4
(ii)
. By
Definition

3.10
,
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\prec F
(as
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
). Hence (

#

‣
3.2
) is satisfied by
{F({u},{i_{1}})}
.

(iii)

Let
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<u
. Then
p<\omega_{i}<\omega_{i}+2p=i_{1}<u<n-1
, which implies
u\nsim\omega_{i}
. Hence
\omega_{i}\nsim i_{1}
implies
{F({u},{n-1})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Using
Corollary

3.41
,
{F({u},{n-1})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
Since
{F({u},{n-1})}\ll F
,
Definition

3.10
implies
{F({u},{n-1})}\prec F
. Hence
{F({u},{n-1})}
satisfies (

#

‣
3.2
).

In each case, there exists
v\in F^{c}
such that
{F({u},{v})}
satisfies (

#

‣
3.2
). Therefore
F
is a spanning facet.
∎

Corollary 3.44
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},n-1\}
. Suppose
\omega_{i}\in\{p+1,p+2,\ldots,\mathbbm{c}-p\}
and
i_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\bigl(\{\omega_{i}-2p\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{i}-2p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{i}-p-2\ (\text{mod $n$})\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i}-p-1,\omega_{i}-p,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-1\}\bigr)
. Then
F
is a spanning facet.

Proof.

Since
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-p
, we have
\omega_{i}+2p-1\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-1
. Using
p\geq 2
,
2\mathbbm{c}\leq n+1
and
\omega_{i}\geq p+1
, it follows that
\omega_{i}-p-1\geq 0
and
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-1\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-p-2\leq n-p-1\leq n-3
.
Therefore
\{\omega_{i}-p-1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{i}-p\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{i}+2p-1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-1\ (\text{mod $n$})\}=\{\omega_{i}-p-1,\omega_{i}-p,\ldots,\omega_{i}+2p-1,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-1\}
.
Observe that
i_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})|-2p\leq t\leq 2p-1\}
. By
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2
. Since
p+1\leq\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-p
, we get
0<3p+1\leq\omega_{i}+2p\leq\mathbbm{c}+p\leq n-2p+2<n
. Hence
\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})=\omega_{i}+2p
. Moreover,
\omega_{i}>p
. By
Proposition

3.43
,
F
is a spanning facet.
∎

Lemma 3.45
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
be such that
F\in\Sigma
. Then
F
is a spanning facet.

Proof.

Since
F\in\Sigma=\Sigma_{1}\sqcup\Sigma_{2}\sqcup\Sigma_{3}
, there exists
i\in[n-2]
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},n-1\}
.
Let
\mathcal{W}:=V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\bigl(\{\omega_{i}-2p\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{i}-2p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{i}-p-2\ (\text{mod $n$})\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i}-p-1,\omega_{i}-p,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-1\}\bigr)
.
We consider three cases: (1)
F\in\Sigma_{1}
, (2)
F\in\Sigma_{2}
, (3)
F\in\Sigma_{3}
.

(1)

Let
F\in\Sigma_{1}
. Then
\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{1}=\{p+1,p+2,\ldots,2p-1\}
and
i_{1}\in V(C_{p}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}=V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\bigr(\{\omega_{i}-2p\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{i}-2p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,n-1\}\sqcup\{0,1,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-1\}\bigl)
.
By
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
\mathbbm{c}\geq 3p-1
, which implies
2p-1\leq\mathbbm{c}-p
. Hence
\omega_{i}\in\{p+1,p+2,\ldots,\mathbbm{c}-p\}
. Observe that
i_{1}\in\mathcal{W}
. Therefore,
F
is a spanning facet by
Corollary

3.44
.

(2)

Let
F\in\Sigma_{2}
. Then
\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{2}=\{2p,2p+1,\ldots,\mathbbm{c}-p\}
and
i_{1}\in V(C_{p}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}=V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus(\{\omega_{i}-2p,\omega_{i}-2p+1,\ldots,\omega_{i}-2\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i},\omega_{i}+1,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-1\}\sqcup\{n-1\})
.

Since
p\geq 2
, we have
p+1<2p
. Hence
\omega_{i}\in\{p+1,p+2,\ldots,\mathbbm{c}-p\}
. Moreover,
2p\leq\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-p
and
\mathbbm{c}\leq n+1
imply
0\leq\omega_{i}-2p\leq\omega_{i}-p-2\leq\mathbbm{c}-2p-2\leq n-2p-1<n-1
. Thus
\{\omega_{i}-2p\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{i}-2p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{i}-p-2\ (\text{mod $n$})\}=\{\omega_{i}-2p,\omega_{i}-2p+1,\ldots,\omega_{i}-p-2\}
. This gives
\mathcal{W}=V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\{\omega_{i}-2p,\omega_{i}-2p+1,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-1\}
.
If
i_{1}\neq\omega_{i}-1
, then
i_{1}\in\mathcal{W}
, and by
Corollary

3.44
,
F
is a spanning facet.

Now, assume
i_{1}=\omega_{i}-1
. We prove that
F
is a spanning facet. For this, it suffices to show that for each
u\in F
, there exists
v\in F^{c}
such that
{F({u},{v})}
satisfies (

#

‣
3.2
). Let
u\in F
. We consider two subcases: (i)
u<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
and (ii)
u\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
.

(i)

u<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}

We have
i_{1}\in F^{c}
and
({F({u},{i_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i},u,n-1\}
. By
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2
. Since
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-p
and
p\geq 2
, we get
\omega_{i}+2p\leq\mathbbm{c}+p\leq n-2p+2\leq n-2<n-1
.
Therefore,
n-1+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})=2p-1<\omega_{i}<n-1-2p
. This implies
\omega_{i}\nsim n-1
, and
u\nsim\omega_{i}
or
u\nsim n-1
. Hence
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Since
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
, we have
\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. Clearly,
u\neq\omega_{i}
. This means that either
u<\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
or
\omega_{i}<u<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
.
First, let
u<\omega_{i}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. Then
u<\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
implies
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
, and hence
({F({u},{i_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{u,n-1\}
.
Using
u\neq i_{1}
and
i_{1}=\omega_{i}-1
, we get
u<i_{1}
. Hence
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\ll F
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
.
Now, let
\omega_{i}<u<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. Since
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
,
Remark

3.2
(iii)
implies
u<_{\Omega}\omega_{i}
. By
Definition

3.4
(ii)
,
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\ll F
. In both cases,
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\ll F
.
By
Corollary

3.41
,
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
(as
\omega_{i}+2p<n-1
). Therefore,
{F({u},{i_{1}})}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
. It follows that
{F({u},{i_{1}})}
satisfies (

#

‣
3.2
).

(ii)

u\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
.

We have
n-1\in F^{c}
and
({F({u},{n-1})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i},i_{1},u\}
. Since
2p\leq\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}-p
, we get
2p-1\leq\omega_{i}-1=i_{1}<\omega_{i}<\omega_{i}+2p\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}\leq u<n-1
. This implies
u\nsim\omega_{i}
and
u\nsim i_{1}
. Hence
{F({u},{n-1})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.

Since
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
. Using
i_{1}<u
, it follows that
({F({u},{n-1})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},u\}
. By
Definition

3.4
(i)
,
{F({u},{n-1})}\ll F
(as
u<n-1
).
Moreover, by
Corollary

3.41
,
u>i_{1}+2p
implies
{F({u},{n-1})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
Therefore
{F({u},{n-1})}\prec F
by
Definition

3.10
. Hence (

#

‣
3.2
) is satisfied by
{F({u},{n-1})}
.

In either case, there exists
v\in F^{c}
such that
{F({u},{v})}
satisfies (

#

‣
3.2
). Thus
F
is a spanning facet.

(3)

Let
F\in\Sigma_{3}
.
We have
\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{3}=\{\mathbbm{c}-p+1,\mathbbm{c}-p+2,\ldots,n-p-1\}
and
i_{1}\in V(C_{p}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}=V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus(\{\mu,\mu+1,\ldots,\omega_{i},\omega_{i}+1,\ldots,\omega_{i}+p\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})|\ p+1\leq t\leq 2p-1\}\sqcup\{\nu\})
, where
\mu=\min\{2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i},\omega_{i}-2p\}
and
\nu=\begin{cases}n-1&\text{ if $\omega_{i}<n-2p-1$},\\
\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})&\text{ if $\omega_{i}\geq n-2p-1$}.\end{cases}

Since
\mu\leq\omega_{i}-2p
, we have
\{\omega_{i}+t|-2p\leq t\leq p\}\subseteq\{\mu,\mu+1,\ldots,\omega_{i},\omega_{i}+1,\ldots,\omega_{i}+p\}
.
By
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
\mathbbm{c}\geq 3p-1
. Using
\mathbbm{c}-p+1\leq\omega_{i}\leq n-p-1
, we obtain
0\leq\mathbbm{c}-3p+1\leq\omega_{i}-2p<\omega_{i}+p\leq n-1
.
This gives
\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})|-2p\leq t\leq p\}=\{\omega_{i}+t|-2p\leq t\leq p\}
. Hence
\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})|-2p\leq t\leq 2p-1\}=\{\omega_{i}+t|-2p\leq t\leq p\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})|\ p+1\leq t\leq 2p-1\}\subseteq\{\mu,\mu+1,\ldots,\omega_{i},\omega_{i}+1,\ldots,\omega_{i}+p\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})|\ p+1\leq t\leq 2p-1\}\sqcup\{\nu\}
.
Therefore,
i_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})|-2p\leq t\leq 2p-1\}
.

Suppose
i_{1}=\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})
. Since
\nu\neq i_{1}
, we have
\nu=n-1
, and hence
\omega_{i}<n-2p-1
. This means that
\omega_{i}+2p\ \text{(mod $n$)}=\omega_{i}+2p
.
Moreover,
\mathbbm{c}\geq 3p-1
and
p\geq 2
implies
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}-p+1\geq 2p>p
.

Hence
F
is a spanning facet by
Proposition

3.43
.

Therefore, we conclude that if
F\in\Sigma
, then
F
is a spanning facet.
∎

We now proceed to prove that if
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
is a spanning facet, then
F\in\Sigma
. Before presenting the proof, we first establish some results.

Proposition 3.46
.

Suppose
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F^{c}=\{u,v,w\}
, where
u,v,w\in V(C_{n}^{p})
and
v,w\in\{u+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),u+2\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,u+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})\}
. Then
F
is not a spanning facet.

Proof.

Observe that
u,v,w\sim u+p
(mod
n
). If
u+p
(mod
n
)
\in F^{c}
, then
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is connected, which contradicts
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Hence
u+p
(mod
n
)
\in F
. For any
x\in F^{c}
,
C_{n}^{p}[({F({u+p\text{ (mod $n$)}},{x})})^{c}]
is connected, and therefore
{F({u+p\text{ (mod $n$)}},{x})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.
Thus there does not exist
x\in F^{c}
such that
{F({u+p\text{ (mod $n$)}},{x})}
satisfies (

#

‣
3.2
). Therefore,
F
is not a spanning facet.
∎

Corollary 3.47
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Suppose
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
. Then
F
is not a spanning facet.

Proof.

By
Proposition

3.40
,
F^{c}=\{u,v,w\}
for some
u,v,w\in V(C_{n}^{p})
with
u<v<w\leq u+2p
. Hence
F
is not a spanning facet by
Proposition

3.46
.
∎

Proposition 3.48
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
be a spanning facet such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Then
\omega_{i}\in\{p+1,p+2,\ldots,n-p-1\}
and
i_{2}=n-1
.

Proof.

By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1},i_{2}
. Hence
\omega_{i}\neq i_{1},i_{2}
.

•

Suppose
\omega_{i}\leq p
. Since
p<\mathbbm{c}
by
Proposition

3.1
(i)
, we have
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
. Moreover,
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-p\geq n-p
(as
2\mathbbm{c}\geq n
).
First, suppose
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
. Then
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected and
\omega_{i}\leq p
imply
i_{2}>\omega_{i}
. Using
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
,
Remark

3.2
(i)
yields
i_{2}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
.
This means that
n-p\leq i_{2}\leq n-1
. Since
0\leq i_{1}<\omega_{i}\leq p
, we get
i_{1},\omega_{i}\in\{i_{2}+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),i_{2}+2\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,i_{2}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})\}
. From
Proposition

3.46
,
F
is not a spanning facet, a contradiction. Hence
i_{1}>\omega_{i}
. By
Remark

3.2
(i)
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
implies
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
, which gives
n-p\leq i_{1}<i_{2}\leq n-1
. Using
0\leq\omega_{i}\leq p
, we obtain
i_{2},\omega_{i}\in\{i_{1}+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),i_{1}+2\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,i_{1}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})\}
. By
Proposition

3.46
,
F
is not a spanning facet, again a contradiction. Therefore
\omega_{i}>p
.

•

Suppose
\omega_{i}\geq n-p
. By
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
n-p>\mathbbm{c}
, which implies
\omega_{i}>\mathbbm{c}
. Note that
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-n+p\leq p+1
(as
2\mathbbm{c}\leq n+1
).

First, suppose
i_{2}>\omega_{i}
. Then
n-p\leq\omega_{i}<i_{2}\leq n-1
. Since
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected,
i_{1}<\omega_{i}
. By
Remark

3.2
(i)
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
implies
i_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
. Hence
0\leq i_{1}\leq p
.

Now, suppose
i_{2}<\omega_{i}
. Then
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{2}
implies
i_{2}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
. Thus
0\leq i_{1}<i_{2}\leq p
. Moreover, we have
n-p\leq\omega_{i}\leq n-1
.

In either case,
i_{1},i_{2}\in\{\omega_{i}+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{i}+2\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})\}
. It follows that
F
is not a spanning facet by
Proposition

3.46
, a contradiction.
Hence
\omega_{i}<n-p
.

We conclude that
\omega_{i}\in\{p+1,p+2,\ldots,n-p-1\}
.

By the definition of
\Omega
, we have
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}n-1
.
Suppose
n-1\in F
. Since
F
is a spanning facet, there exists
v\in F^{c}
such that
{F({n-1},{v})}
satisfies (

#

‣
3.2
). This means that
{F({n-1},{v})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F({n-1},{v})}\prec F
. Since
F
is a spanning facet,
F\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Corollary

3.47
.
Therefore, if
F\ll{F({n-1},{v})}
, then
F\prec{F({n-1},{v})}
by
Definition

3.10
, a contradiction. Hence
{F({n-1},{v})}\ll F
.

If
v=\omega_{i}
, then
({F({n-1},{v})})^{c}=({F({n-1},{\omega_{i}})})^{c}=\{i_{1},i_{2},n-1\}
. Since
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1},i_{2},n-1
,
Definition

3.4
(ii)
yields
F\ll{F({n-1},{\omega_{i}})}
, a contradiction. Thus
v\in\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Using
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1},i_{2},n-1
and
i_{1}<i_{2}<n-1
, we have either
({F({n-1},{v})})^{c}=({F({n-1},{i_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{2},n-1\}
or
({F({n-1},{v})})^{c}=({F({n-1},{i_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},n-1\}
.
By
Definition

3.4
(i)
,
i_{1}<i_{2}
implies
F\ll{F({n-1},{i_{1}})}
, and
i_{2}<n-1
implies
F\ll{F({n-1},{i_{2}})}
, again a contradiction. Hence
n-1\in F^{c}
.

Clearly,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}n-1
and
i_{1}<i_{2}
imply
i_{2}=n-1
.
∎

Proposition 3.49
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Suppose there exists
u\in F
with
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u
and
i_{1}<u
. If either
{F({u},{i_{2}})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, or
{F({u},{i_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
{F({u},{i_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
, then
F
is not a spanning facet.

Proof.

Suppose
F
is a spanning facet. Since
u\in F
, there exists
v\in F^{c}
such that
{F({u},{v})}
satisfies (

#

‣
3.2
). This means that
{F({u},{v})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F({u},{v})}\prec F
. By
Proposition

3.48
,
i_{2}=n-1
, and hence
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},n-1\}
. By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1},n-1
. Moreover,
F\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Corollary

3.47
. Since
{F({u},{v})}\prec F
,
Definition

3.10
implies
{F({u},{v})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
{F({u},{v})}\ll F
.

First, suppose
v=\omega_{i}
. Then
({F({u},{v})})^{c}=({F({u},{\omega_{i}})})^{c}=\{i_{1},n-1,u\}
. By
Definition

3.4
(ii)
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1},n-1,u
implies
F\ll{F({u},{\omega_{i}})}
, a contradiction. Now, suppose
v=i_{1}
. Then
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}u,n-1
and
u<n-1
imply
({F({u},{v})})^{c}=({F({u},{i_{1}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{u,n-1\}
.
Since
i_{1}<u
, we get
F\ll{F({u},{i_{1}})}
by
Definition

3.4
(i)
, again a contradiction.
Hence
v=i_{2}=n-1
. We get
{F({u},{i_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. By assumption,
{F({u},{i_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in[p-1]
, a contradiction to
{F({u},{v})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

Hence, there does not exist any
v\in F^{c}
such that
{F({u},{v})}
satisfies (

#

‣
3.2
). Therefore
F
is not a spanning facet.
∎

Proposition 3.50
.

Let
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
such that
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. Suppose
F
is a spanning facet. We have the following.

(i)

If
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}
, then
i_{1}\notin\{\omega_{i}-2p,\omega_{i}-2p+1,\ldots,\omega_{i}-2\}
.

(ii)

i_{1}\notin\{\omega_{i}+t\ |\ 1\leq t\leq p\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})\ |\ p+1\leq t\leq 2p-1\}
.

(iii)

Let
\nu:=\begin{cases}n-1&\text{ if $\omega_{i}<n-2p-1$},\\
\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})&\text{ if $\omega_{i}\geq n-2p-1$}.\end{cases}
Then
i_{1}\neq\nu
.

Proof.

By
Remark

3.3
,
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
. Since
F
is a spanning facet,
i_{2}=n-1
by
Proposition

3.48
.

(i)

We have
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}
. By
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
\mathbbm{c}<n-p
. Thus,
p\geq 2
implies
\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}<n-1
.

First, suppose
i_{1}\in\{\omega_{i}-2p,\omega_{i}-2p+1,\ldots,\omega_{i}-p-1\}
. Then
i_{1}<i_{1}+p\leq\omega_{i}-1<\omega_{i}<n-1=i_{2}
and
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}-2p
. Since
i+p\neq\omega_{i},i_{1},i_{2}
, we get
i_{1}+p\in F
. Note that
({F({i_{1}+p},{i_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i},i_{1},i_{1}+p\}
. We have
i_{1}<i_{1}+p<\omega_{i}\leq i_{1}+2p
, which implies
i_{1}\sim(i_{1}+p)
and
(i_{1}+p)\sim\omega_{i}
. Thus
{F({i_{1}+p},{i_{2}})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Moreover,
i_{1}+p<\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}
implies
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}+p
.
From
Proposition

3.49
(for
u=i_{1}+p
),
F
is not a spanning facet, a contradiction. Hence
i_{1}\notin\{\omega_{i}-2p,\omega_{i}-2p+1,\ldots,\omega_{i}-p-1\}
.

Now, suppose
i_{1}\in\{\omega_{i}-p,\omega_{i}-p+1,\ldots,\omega_{i}-2\}
. Then
i_{1}<i_{1}+1\leq\omega_{i}-1<\omega_{i}<n-1=i_{2}
, which implies
i_{1}+1\in F
. We have
({F({i_{1}+1},{i_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i},i_{1},i_{1}+1\}
. Since
\omega_{i}-p\leq i_{1}<i_{1}+1<\omega_{i}
, it follows that
{F({i_{1}+1},{i_{2}})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
.
Moreover,
i_{1}+1<\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}
implies
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}+1
. Therefore
F
is not a spanning facet by
Proposition

3.49
(for
u=i_{1}+1
), again a contradiction. Hence
i_{1}\notin\{\omega_{i}-p,\omega_{i}-p+1,\ldots,\omega_{i}-2\}
.

Therefore
i_{1}\notin\{\omega_{i}-2p,\omega_{i}-2p+1,\ldots,\omega_{i}-2\}
.

(ii)

Suppose
i_{1}\in\{\omega_{i}+t\ |\ 1\leq t\leq p\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})\ |\ p+1\leq t\leq 2p-1\}
.

Since
F
is a spanning facet, we have
p+1\leq\omega_{i}\leq n-p-1
by
Proposition

3.48
.
It follows that
\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})\ |\ 1\leq t\leq p\}=\{\omega_{i}+t\ |\ 1\leq t\leq p\}
. Hence
i_{1}\in\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})\ |\ 1\leq t\leq 2p-1\}
.
Observe that if
\omega_{i}+2p\geq n-1
, then
i_{1}<i_{2}=n-1
implies
i_{2}\in\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})\ |\ 2\leq t\leq 2p\}
. By
Proposition

3.46
,
F
is not a spanning facet, a contradiction. So,
\omega_{i}+2p<n-1
.

This implies
\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})\ |\ p+1\leq t\leq 2p-1\}=\{\omega_{i}+t\ |\ p+1\leq t\leq 2p-1\}
, and therefore
i_{1}\in\{\omega_{i}+t\ |\ 1\leq t\leq 2p-1\}
. Thus
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{1}+1\leq\omega_{i}+2p<n-1
, which implies
i_{1}+1\in F
.

If
\omega_{i}<\mathbbm{c}
, then
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}
and
i_{1}>\omega_{i}
imply
i_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
, and hence
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}+1
. If
\omega_{i}\geq\mathbbm{c}
, then
i_{1}+1>\omega_{i}
implies
\omega_{i}<_{\Omega}i_{1}+1
.
Since
F
is a spanning facet,
Proposition

3.49
implies
{F({i_{1}+1},{i_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F({i_{1}+1},{i_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

We have
({F({i_{1}+1},{i_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},i_{1}+1\}
with
\omega_{i}<i_{1}<i_{1}+1
. Since
C_{n}^{p}[({F({i_{1}+1},{i_{2}})})^{c}]
is disconnected,
i_{1}\geq\omega_{i}+p+1
. Hence
i_{1}\leq\omega_{i}+2p-1
implies
i_{1}+1=\omega_{i}+2p-\gamma+1
for some
\gamma\in[p-1]
.
Since
\omega_{i}\leq n-2p-2
,
Proposition

3.12
(9)
(9)(i)
yields
{F({i_{1}+1},{i_{2}})}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, a contradiction.

Therefore
i_{1}\notin\{\omega_{i}+1,\omega_{i}+2,\ldots,\omega_{i}+p\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i}+p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{i}+p+2\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{i}+2p-1\ (\text{mod $n$})\}
.

(iii)

Suppose
i_{1}=\nu
. Since
i_{1}<i_{2}=n-1
, we have
i_{1}\neq n-1
. Note that if
\omega_{i}\leq n-2p-1
, then
\nu=n-1
. Hence
\omega_{i}\geq n-2p
. This implies
i_{1}=\nu=\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})
.
Since
\omega_{i}<n-1=i_{2}
and
\omega_{i}+2p\geq n>n-1=i_{2}
, it follows that
i_{2}\in\{\omega_{i}+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{i}+2\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{i}+2p-1\ (\text{mod $n$})\}
. By
Proposition

3.46
,
F
is not a spanning facet, a contradiction. Thus
i_{1}\neq\nu
.

∎

Lemma 3.51
.

Suppose
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
is a spanning facet. Then
F\in\Sigma
.

Proof.

We have
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Suppose
F\in\mathcal{A}_{j}
such that
F^{c}=\{\omega_{j}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
. Since
F
is a spanning facet,
Proposition

3.48
implies
\omega_{j}\in\{p+1,p+2,\ldots,n-p-1\}
and
j_{2}=n-1
.

In this proof, for each
\omega_{j}
, we first identify all possible values of
j_{1}
for which
F
is a spanning facet. Then, we show that for all such values of
j_{1}
corresponding to a given
\omega_{j}
,
F\in\Sigma_{m}
for some
m\in[3]
, thereby implying that
F\in\Sigma
.
Before proceeding further, recall that
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{1}
by
Remark

3.3
.

Let

\nu:=\begin{cases}n-1&\text{ if $\omega_{j}<n-2p-1$},\\
\omega_{j}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})&\text{ if $\omega_{j}\geq n-2p-1$}.\end{cases}

Based on the values of
\omega_{j}
, we consider the following four cases.

(i)

\omega_{j}\in\{p+1,p+2,\ldots,2p-1\}
.

By
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
\mathbbm{c}\geq 3p-1
. We have
\omega_{j}\leq 2p-1<3p-1\leq\mathbbm{c}
and
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{1}
. By
Remark

3.2
(i)
, either
j_{1}<\omega_{j}
or
j_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
.
Suppose
j_{1}<\omega_{j}
. Then
0\leq j_{1}<\omega_{j}<n-1=j_{2}
. Since
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected,
j_{2}\nsim j_{1}
or
j_{1}\nsim\omega_{j}
. It follows that
\omega_{j}>j_{2}+2p\ (\text{mod $n$})=2p-1\geq\omega_{j}
, a contradiction.
Hence
j_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
, and therefore
j_{1}\notin\{0,1,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-1\}
.

Since
C_{n}^{p}[F^{c}]
is disconnected,
j_{2}\nsim\omega_{j}
or
\omega_{j}\nsim j_{1}
. Using
p+1\leq\omega_{j}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}\leq j_{1}<n-1=j_{2}
, we obtain
j_{1}<\omega_{j}-2p\ (\text{mod $n$})\leq n-1
. This implies
j_{1}\notin\{\omega_{j}-2p\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{j}-2p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,n-1\}
.

Hence
j_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\bigl(\{\omega_{j}-2p\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{j}-2p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,n-1\}\sqcup\{0,1,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-1\}\bigr)
. Note that
\omega_{j}\in\mathcal{U}_{1}
and
j_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{j}}
. Thus
F\in\Sigma_{1}
.

(ii)

\omega_{j}\in\{2p,2p+1,\ldots,\mathbbm{c}-p\}
.

Since
\omega_{j}\leq\mathbbm{c}-p<\mathbbm{c}
and
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{1}
, either
j_{1}<\omega_{j}
or
j_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
. Hence
j_{1}\notin\{\omega_{j},\omega_{j}+1,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-1\}
. Moreover,
j_{1}<j_{2}=n-1
implies
j_{1}\neq n-1
.
Since
F
is a spanning facet and
\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}
, by
Proposition

3.50
(i)
,
j_{1}\notin\{\omega_{j}-2p,\omega_{j}-2p+1,\ldots,\omega_{j}-2\}
.

Therefore,
j_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\bigl(\{\omega_{j}-2p,\omega_{j}-2p+1,\ldots,\omega_{j}-2\}\sqcup\{\omega_{j},\omega_{j}+1,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-1\}\sqcup\{n-1\}\bigr)
. Here,
\omega_{j}\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
and
j_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{j}}
, which implies that
F\in\Sigma_{2}
.

(iii)

\omega_{j}\in\{\mathbbm{c}-p+1,\mathbbm{c}-p+2,\ldots,\mathbbm{c}\}
.

Since
F
is a spanning facet and
\omega_{j}\leq\mathbbm{c}
,
j_{1}\notin\{\omega_{j}-2p,\omega_{j}-2p+1,\ldots,\omega_{j}-2\}
by
Proposition

3.50
(i)
.

We now prove that
j_{1}\neq\omega_{j}-1
. Suppose
j_{1}=\omega_{j}-1
. By
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2
. Hence
j_{1}=\omega_{j}-1<\omega_{j}\leq\mathbbm{c}<\mathbbm{c}+p\leq n-2p+2<n-1=j_{2}\implies\mathbbm{c}+p\in F
.
We have
({F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{j},\omega_{j}-1,\mathbbm{c}+p\}
. If
\omega_{j}=\mathbbm{c}
, then
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}\mathbbm{c}+p
and
{F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. By
Proposition

3.49
(for
u=\mathbbm{c}+p
),
F
is not a spanning facet, a contradiction. Hence
\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}
.
Since
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}-p
,
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}+p
. So
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}\mathbbm{c}+p
by
Remark

3.2
(i)
.
Using the fact that
F
is a spanning facet,
Proposition

3.49
yields
{F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
with
{F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

Note that
({F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{j}\}\sqcup\{\omega_{j}-1,\mathbbm{c}+p\}
with
j_{1}=\omega_{j}-1<\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}+p
.
Since
\mathbbm{c}-p+1\leq\omega_{j}\leq\mathbbm{c}-1
,
j_{1}=\omega_{j}-1
implies
j_{1}+p+2\leq\mathbbm{c}+p\leq j_{1}+2p
. This means that
\mathbbm{c}+p=j_{1}+2p-\gamma+1
for some
\gamma\in[p-1]
.
First, suppose
\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
. Then
{F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(3)
(3)(i)
, a contradiction.
Now, suppose
\omega_{j}\geq\mathbbm{c}-\frac{p}{2}
. We have
\omega_{j}-p<\omega_{j}-1=j_{1}<\omega_{j}
. Since
\mathbbm{c}+p=j_{1}+2p-\gamma+1
with
\gamma\in[p-1]
, if
j_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p-1
, then
{F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(4)
(4)(i)
, a contradiction.
This implies
j_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p
. Since
\mathbbm{c}-p+1\leq\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}
, we have
\omega_{j}=\mathbbm{c}-\gamma
for some
\gamma\in[p-1]
. Moreover,
\mathbbm{c}+p=2\mathbbm{c}-(\mathbbm{c}-\gamma)+p-\gamma=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}+p-\gamma
.
By
Proposition

3.12
(5)
(5)(i)
,
{F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, again a contradiction. Hence
j_{1}\neq\omega_{j}-1
.

Since
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{1}
, we have
j_{1}\neq\omega_{j}
. By
Proposition

3.50
(ii)
and
(iii)
,
j_{1}\notin\{\omega_{j}+1,\omega_{j}+2,\ldots,\omega_{j}+p\}\sqcup\{\omega_{j}+p+1\ (\text{mod \ $n$}),\omega_{j}+p+2\ (\text{mod \ $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{j}+2p-1\ (\text{mod \ $n$})\}\sqcup\{\nu\}
.

Therefore,
j_{1}\notin\{\omega_{j}-2p,\omega_{j}-2p+1,\ldots,\omega_{j},\omega_{j}+1,\ldots,\omega_{j}+p\}\sqcup\{\omega_{j}+p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{j}+p+2\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{j}+2p-1\ (\text{mod $n$})\}\sqcup\{\nu\}
.
Since
\omega_{j}-2p\leq\mathbbm{c}-2p<\mathbbm{c}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
implies
\min\{2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j},\omega_{j}-2p\}=\omega_{j}-2p
and
\omega_{j}\in\mathcal{U}_{3}
, we have
j_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{j}}
. Hence
F\in\Sigma_{3}
.

(iv)

\omega_{j}\in\{\mathbbm{c}+1,\mathbbm{c}+2,\ldots,n-p-1\}
.

In this case,
\omega_{j}\in\mathcal{U}_{3}
. Since
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
and
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{1}
, by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
,
j_{1}\notin\{2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j},2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}+1,\ldots,\omega_{j}\}
.
Moreover, by
Proposition

3.50
(ii)
and
(iii)
,
j_{1}\notin\{\omega_{j}+1,\omega_{j}+2,\ldots,\omega_{j}+p\}\sqcup\{\omega_{j}+p+1\ (\text{mod \ $n$}),\omega_{j}+p+2\ (\text{mod \ $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{j}+2p-1\ (\text{mod \ $n$})\}\sqcup\{\nu\}
.
Therefore,
j_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus(\{2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j},2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}+1,\ldots,\omega_{j},\omega_{j}+1,\ldots,\omega_{j}+p\}\sqcup\{\omega_{j}+p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{j}+p+2\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,\omega_{j}+2p-1\ (\text{mod $n$})\}\sqcup\{\nu\})
.

First, assume
\mathbbm{c}+p\leq\omega_{j}\leq n-p-1
. Then
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}\leq\mathbbm{c}-p\leq\omega_{j}-2p
, which implies
\min\{2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j},\omega_{j}-2p\}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
.
Since
\omega_{j}\in\mathcal{U}_{3}
, we have
j_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{j}}
. Hence
F\in\Sigma_{3}
.

Now, assume
\mathbbm{c}+1\leq\omega_{j}\leq\mathbbm{c}+p-1
.
Then
\omega_{j}-2p\leq\mathbbm{c}-p-1<\mathbbm{c}-p+1\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
. Hence
\min\{2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j},\omega_{j}-2p\}=\omega_{j}-2p
. Since
\omega_{j}\in\mathcal{U}_{3}
, we aim to prove that
F\in\Sigma_{3}
. For this we need to show that
j_{1}\in V(C_{n}^{p})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{j}}
. Observe that it suffices to show that
j_{1}\notin\{\omega_{j}-2p,\omega_{j}-2p+1,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-1\}
.

•

Suppose
j_{1}\in\{\omega_{j}-2p,\omega_{j}-2p+1,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-2\}
. Since
\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2
by
Proposition

3.1
(i)
and
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
, we get
j_{1}<j_{1}+1\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-1<\mathbbm{c}<\omega_{j}\leq\mathbbm{c}+p-1\leq n-2p+1<n-1=j_{2}
. This gives
j_{1}+1\in F
.
By
Remark

3.2
(ii)
,
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
and
j_{1}+1<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}
imply
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{1}+1
.
Since
F
is a spanning facet,
Proposition

3.49
(for
u=j_{1}+1
) yields
{F({j_{1}+1},{j_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F({j_{1}+1},{j_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
. We have
({F({j_{1}+1},{j_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{j}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},j_{1}+1\}
with
j_{1}<j_{1}+1<\omega_{j}
.
Since
{F({j_{1}+1},{j_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
, it follows that
j_{1}\leq\omega_{j}-p-2
. Then
j_{1}\geq\omega_{j}-2p
implies
j_{1}=\omega_{j}-2p+\gamma-1
for some
\gamma\in[p-1]
. Therefore, if
\omega_{j}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
{F({j_{1}+1},{j_{2}})}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(2)
(2)(i)
, a contradiction. So, assume
\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.

Suppose
j_{1}\geq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p
. Then
j_{1}\leq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-2
implies
j_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p+\gamma-1
for some
1\leq\gamma\leq p-1
.
Therefore,
Proposition

3.12
(1)
(1)(i)
yields
{F({j_{1}+1},{j_{2}})}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, a contradiction. Hence
j_{1}<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-p
.

It follows that
j_{1}<j_{1}+p<2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}<\omega_{j}<n-1=j_{2}
, which gives
j_{1}+p\in F
.
We have
({F({j_{1}+p},{j_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{j},j_{1},j_{1}+p\}
. Using
j_{1}<j_{1}+p<\omega_{j}\leq j_{1}+2p
(as
j_{1}\geq\omega_{j}-2p
), we obtain
j_{1}\sim(j_{1}+p)
and
(j_{1}+p)\sim\omega_{j}
. Hence
{F({j_{1}+p},{j_{2}})}\notin M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
. Moreover,
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
implies
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}j_{1}+p
by
Remark

3.2
(ii)
.
By
Proposition

3.49
(for
u=j_{1}+p
),
F
is not a spanning facet, a contradiction. Therefore
j_{1}\notin\{\omega_{j}-2p,\omega_{j}-2p+1,\ldots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-2\}
.

•

Suppose
j_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-1
. By
Proposition

3.1
(i)
,
\mathbbm{c}\leq n-3p+2
. It follows that
j_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-1<\mathbbm{c}<\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}+p\leq n-2p+2<n-1=j_{2}
. Hence
\mathbbm{c}+p\in F
.
Since
F
is a spanning facet,
{F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})}\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
and
{F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})}\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.49
(for
u=\mathbbm{c}+p
).
Using
\mathbbm{c}<\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}+p
, we get
\omega_{j}<_{\Omega}\mathbbm{c}+p
. This implies
({F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})})^{c}=\{\omega_{j}\}\sqcup\{j_{1},\mathbbm{c}+p\}
with
j_{1}<\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}+p
.
Since
\mathbbm{c}+1\leq\omega_{j}\leq\mathbbm{c}+p-1
and
j_{1}=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-1
, we have
\mathbbm{c}-p\leq j_{1}\leq\mathbbm{c}-2
, which implies
j_{1}+p+2\leq\mathbbm{c}+p\leq j_{1}+2p
.
This means that
j_{1}=(\mathbbm{c}+p)-2p+\gamma-1
, where
\gamma\in[p-1]
. Therefore, if
\omega_{j}\geq\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
, then
{F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
Proposition

3.12
(8)
(8)(i)
, a contradiction.
So, let
\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}+\frac{p}{2}
.
Since
\omega_{j}>\mathbbm{c}
, we get
2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}+p<\mathbbm{c}+p<\omega_{j}+p
. We have
\omega_{j}<\mathbbm{c}+p
, and
j_{1}=(\mathbbm{c}+p)-2p+\gamma-1
, where
\gamma\in[p-1]
.
By
Proposition

3.12
(7)
(7)(i)
,
{F({\mathbbm{c}+p},{j_{2}})}\notin\mathcal{M}_{0}
, again a contradiction. Hence
j_{1}\neq 2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{j}-1
.

In all four cases,
F\in\Sigma_{m}
for some
m\in[3]
. Hence
F\in\Sigma
.
∎

3.2.1.
Counting of Spanning Facets

From
Lemmas

3.45
and
3.51
, a facet
F\in M(\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p}))
is spanning for the shelling order
\prec
on
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
if and only if
F\in\Sigma
. Since
\Sigma=\Sigma_{1}\sqcup\Sigma_{2}\sqcup\Sigma_{3}
, the total number of spanning facets is
|\Sigma_{1}|+|\Sigma_{2}|+|\Sigma_{3}|
.

Recall that for
m\in[3]
, a facet
F\in\Sigma_{m}
if and only if
F\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
for some
i\in[n-2]
such that
F^{c}=\{\omega_{i}\}\sqcup\{i_{1},n-1\}
,
\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{m}
and
i_{1}\in V(C_{p}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}
.

(i)

Let
F\in\Sigma_{1}
. Then
\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{1}=\{p+1,p+2,\dots,2p-1\}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}=\{\omega_{i}-2p\ (\text{mod}\ n),\omega_{i}-2p+1\ (\text{mod}\ n),\dots,n-1\}\sqcup\{0,1,\dots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-1\}
.
Since
\omega_{i}\leq 2p-1
, we have
\{\omega_{i}-2p\ (\text{mod $n$}),\omega_{i}-2p+1\ (\text{mod $n$}),\ldots,n-1\}=\{n+\omega_{i}-2p,n+\omega_{i}-2p+1,\ldots,n-1\}
. It follows that
|\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}|=(2p-\omega_{i})+(2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i})=2\mathbbm{c}+2p-2\omega_{i}
and hence
|V(C_{p}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}|=n-(2\mathbbm{c}+2p-2\omega_{i})
. Moreover,
|\mathcal{U}_{1}|=p-1
. This implies

\displaystyle|\Sigma_{1}|

\displaystyle=(p-1)(n-2\mathbbm{c}-2p)+\left(2\textstyle\sum\limits_{\omega_{i}=p+1}^{2p-1}\omega_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=(p-1)(n-2\mathbbm{c}-2p)+(2p-1)(2p)-p(p+1)=p^{2}+np-2\mathbbm{c}p-p-n+2\mathbbm{c}.

(ii)

Let
F\in\Sigma_{2}
. Then
\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{2}=\{2p,2p+1,\dots,\mathbbm{c}-p\}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}=\{\omega_{i}-2p,\omega_{i}-2p+1,\dots,\omega_{i}-2\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i},\omega_{i}+1,\dots,2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}-1\}\sqcup\{n-1\}
.
We have
|\mathcal{U}_{2}|=\mathbbm{c}-3p+1
and
|V(C_{p}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}|=n-(2\mathbbm{c}+2p-2\omega_{i})
. Hence

\displaystyle|\Sigma_{2}|

\displaystyle=(\mathbbm{c}-3p+1)(n-2\mathbbm{c}-2p)+\left(2\textstyle\sum\limits_{\omega_{i}=2p}^{\mathbbm{c}-p}\omega_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=(\mathbbm{c}-3p+1)(n-2\mathbbm{c}-2p)+(\mathbbm{c}-p)(\mathbbm{c}-p+1)-(2p-1)(2p)

\displaystyle=3p^{2}-\mathbbm{c}^{2}+\mathbbm{c}n+2\mathbbm{c}p-3np+n-p-\mathbbm{c}.

(iii)

Let
F\in\Sigma_{3}
. Then
\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{3}=\{\mathbbm{c}-p+1,\mathbbm{c}-p+2,\dots,n-p-1\}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}=\{\mu,\mu+1,\dots,\omega_{i},\omega_{i}+1,\dots,\omega_{i}+p\}\sqcup\{\omega_{i}+t\ (\text{mod $n$})|\ p+1\leq t\leq 2p-1\}\sqcup\{\nu\}
,
where
\mu=\min\{2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i},\omega_{i}-2p\}
and
\nu=\begin{cases}n-1&\text{if }\omega_{i}<n-2p-1,\\
\omega_{i}+2p\ (\text{mod}\ n)&\text{if }\omega_{i}\geq n-2p-1.\end{cases}

If
\mathbbm{c}-p+1\leq\omega_{i}\leq\mathbbm{c}+p-1
, then there are
2p-1
possibilities for
\omega_{i}
, and
\mu=\omega_{i}-2p
. This implies
|V(C_{p}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}|=n-(4p+1)
.
If
\mathbbm{c}+p\leq\omega_{i}\leq n-p-1
, then there are
n-\mathbbm{c}-2p
possibilities for
\omega_{i}
, and
\mu=2\mathbbm{c}-\omega_{i}
, which implies
|V(C_{p}^{n})\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{i}}|=n-(2\omega_{i}-2\mathbbm{c}+2p+1)
. Therefore

\displaystyle|\Sigma_{3}|

\displaystyle=(2p-1)(n-4p-1)+(n-\mathbbm{c}-2p)(n+2\mathbbm{c}-2p-1)-\left(2\textstyle\sum\limits_{\omega_{i}=\mathbbm{c}+p}^{n-p-1}\omega_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=(2p-1)(n-4p-1)+(n-\mathbbm{c}-2p)(n+2\mathbbm{c}-2p-1)-(n-p-1)(n-p)

\displaystyle\quad+(\mathbbm{c}+p-1)(\mathbbm{c}+p)

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{c}n-\mathbbm{c}^{2}-4p^{2}-n+2p+1.

Total number of spanning facets for the shelling order
\prec

\displaystyle=|\Sigma_{1}|+|\Sigma_{2}|+|\Sigma_{3}|

\displaystyle=(p^{2}+np-2\mathbbm{c}p-p-n+2\mathbbm{c})+(3p^{2}-\mathbbm{c}^{2}+\mathbbm{c}n+2\mathbbm{c}p-3np+n-p-\mathbbm{c})

\displaystyle\quad+(\mathbbm{c}n-\mathbbm{c}^{2}-4p^{2}-n+2p+1)

\displaystyle=2\mathbbm{c}n-2\mathbbm{c}^{2}-2np+\mathbbm{c}-n+1=\frac{n^{2}-4np-n+2}{2}=\binom{n-2p}{2}-(2p^{2}+p-1).

Proof of
Theorem

1.4
.

In
Section

3.1
, we established that
\prec
is a shelling order on
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
for
p\geq 2
and
n\geq 6p-3
. Therefore,
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
is shellable.
In
Section

3.2.1
, we determined that
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
has exactly
\binom{n-2p}{2}-(2p^{2}+p-1)
spanning facets with respect to the shelling order
\prec
.

By
Theorem

2.3
,
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})\simeq\bigvee\limits_{\binom{n-2p}{2}-(2p^{2}+p-1)}\mathbb{S}^{n-4}.

∎

4.
Conclusion and Future Directions

Bravo
[
15
]
proved that the complex
\Delta_{2}(C_{n}^{p})
is not shellable. In
[
19
, Conjecture 4.2]
, the authors conjectured that
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
is not shellable for
2p+3\leq n\leq 4p
(for
n\leq 2p+2
,
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
is void), and shellable for
n\geq 4p+1
.
In this article, we proved that for
p\geq 2
and
n\geq 6p-3
,
\Delta_{3}(C_{n}^{p})
is shellable.

Furthermore, based on SageMath computations for
k=4,5,6
, the authors in
[
19
]
raised the question of whether the complexes
\Delta_{k}(C_{n}^{p})
, if nonvoid, are not shellable for
k\geq 4
and
p\geq 3
.

Since
C_{n}^{p}
is a circulant graph on the generating set
\{1,2,\ldots,p\}
and hence a Cayley graph of
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, the following questions naturally arise.

Question 4.1
.

What can be said about the shellability of
3
-cut complexes of general circulant graphs?

Question 4.2
.

For which classes of Cayley graphs are the cut complexes shellable?
